  H5: ##### 7.	It would be very desirable that the pastors on the same day, after the placing of the Shroud, preach to the people at a convenient time a word on the passion and death of Christ. (under ### 4.1.19 Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of the Sixth Week of Great Lent)
  H5: ##### 8.	It is also entrusted to the same pastors to celebrate on this Friday, after sunset, Tomb Matins in church, in view of the divine service of this day and in view of the solemn rites celebrated at its end. (under ### 4.1.19 Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of the Sixth Week of Great Lent)
  H5: ##### 9.	On Great Saturday itself it is fitting to sing the Liturgy of St. Basil with Vespers. (under ### 4.1.19 Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of the Sixth Week of Great Lent)
  H5: ##### 10.	On the Sunday of the Resurrection very early it is fitting to sing Matins with all the rites that precede and accompany it, according to the rubric approved by this Synod. In cathedral churches, similarly as in non-cathedral ones, this Matins is usually anticipated and celebrated on Saturday evening; the Synod considers that such a custom may be adhered to. (under ### 4.1.19 Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of the Sixth Week of Great Lent)
  H5: ##### 11.	On the first three days of Bright Week it is fitting daily to sing Matins, Liturgy and Vespers in church. (under ### 4.1.19 Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of the Sixth Week of Great Lent)
  H5: ##### 12.	The Synod forbids all priests read liturgies throughout the whole Passion Week, and on the first three days of this week prescribes only the Liturgy of the Presanctified; on Great Thursday and Saturday - only the sung Liturgy, that is of St. Basil the Great with Vespers (Title IV, Chapter I. 3. 5). On Great Friday, with the exception of the feast of the Annunciation, the Liturgy should not be celebrated. (under ### 4.1.19 Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of the Sixth Week of Great Lent)
  H5: ##### 1.	Kathisma proper. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	On "Lord, I have cried" - 10 stichera: Idiomelon of the day twice, Martyricon once, then 7 of the feast, Glory, Both now: of the feast. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 3.	At "O Gladsome Light" - Prokimenon of the Triodion and one reading of the Triodion, then 2nd Prokimenon, 2nd reading of the Triodion and everything else of the Presanctified. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "God is the Lord": Troparion of the feast twice, Glory, Both now: the same. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	After the 1st and 2nd Kathismata - Sessional Hymn of the feast, however, after the 2nd Kathisma, before the Sessional Hymn, we sing "The Choir of angels" exactly as on Sunday; and after the Sessional Hymn - "The Resurrection of Christ", Psalm 50 and immediately Canon. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canons of the feast - two: 1st - with heirmos on 8 and 2nd - on 6. From the 6th Ode - tetraodion, heirmos - twice, troparia - on 6, then the other canon on 6. Katavasia after each ode - Heirmos of the 2nd Canon. Refrain to the troparia - "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee". After the 3rd Ode - Sessional Hymn, after the 6th - Kontakion and Ikos of the feast; at the 9th we do not sing "More honorable"; after the 9th we do not say "It is truly meet" until the Sunday of Ap. Thomas; after the 9th - Exaposteilarion of the feast, however, before the Exaposteilarion we sing "Holy is the Lord our God" three times. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 4.	Stichera of the Praises - 8: all - of the feast, with two Sunday verses, as every Sunday; Glory: of the feast, Both now: "Most blessed Art Thou". (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the Great Doxology - Troparion of the feast only. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H5: ##### 6.	Dismissal specific. (under ### 4.2.1 Lazarus Saturday)
  H4: #### AT Great Matins (under ### 4.2.2 Flower Sunday (Palm Sunday))
  H5: ##### 1.	On "God is the Lord": Troparion of Lazarus twice, Glory, Both now: of Flowery [Sunday]. (under ### 4.2.2 Flower Sunday (Palm Sunday))
  H5: ##### 2.	The blessing and distribution of branches takes place, according to the rubric, immediately after the 50th Psalm, during the kissing of the Gospel. According to our custom, the blessing takes place after the 50th Psalm, and the distribution - during the anointing with oil (myrovanie), that is during the canon. And it takes place thus: When the Priest has sung the Gospel, he places it on the analogion near the tetrapod, between two candles lit on candlesticks. On the tetrapod are placed branches and prepared censers and sprinklers. The Priest approaches the tetrapod and at the time when the Choir reads the 50th Psalm, having put incense in and blessed the censer, censes the branches from the four sides of the tetrapod and, after the conclusion of the 50th Psalm, when the Deacon exclaims (if there be none, then the Priest himself) "Let us pray to the Lord", reads aloud (from the Sluzhebnik (lit. "Service Book") or from the Triodion[^563] ) the first prayer "O Lord our God", and the Choir responds: "Amen". Priest: "Peace be to all"; deacon: "Bow your heads unto the Lord"; and, after "To Thee, O Lord", the Priest reads the second prayer "O Lord God Almighty... unto ages of ages"[^564]; choir: "Amen"; the Priest sprinkles the branches with holy water, saying: "These branches are blessed and sanctified" and the rest; and, after singing the litany "Save, O God", with His right hand anoints the people, and with His left hand distributes to all the branches, which the Deacon solemnly hands to him, and if there be no deacon, then one of the church servers[^565]. (under ### 4.2.2 Flower Sunday (Palm Sunday))
  H5: ##### 1.	The sacred vestments of these days and of the whole Passion Week, beginning from Flowery Sunday evening, should be of violet color. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	All these three days have one form of divine service, that is Lenten, as in the Forty Days [Lent], only with certain exceptions, as given below: (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "Lord, I have cried" - stichera of the Triodion on 6, Glory: Triodion, Both now: Triodion. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Entrance, Prokimenon of the day ("Bless the Lord now") and immediately "Vouchsafe, O Lord". (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Aposticha of the Triodion with specific verses. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 4.	After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart" with the Trisagion - Troparion "Virgin Theotokos" and everything else with prostrations, as on the previous Sundays of Great Lent. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 5.	Dismissal specific, so also on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday and Thursday at the end of the Hours: "Christ the Lord, Who goes"[^567]. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "Lord, I have cried" - 10 stichera: all - of the Triodion, Glory, Both now: Triodion. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Entrance with the Gospel and, after "O Gladsome Light", - two Prokimena of the Triodion, each - with its reading. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 3.	After "Let my prayer be set forth" the Priest: "And that we may be accounted worthy" and the rest and reads the Gospel of Matthew, Section 98 and the rest of the Presanctified with the dismissal "Christ the Lord, Who goes". (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H4: #### AT COMPLINE (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 1.	At Alleluia, instead of the Trinity Hymns, we sing the Troparion "Behold the Bridegroom" twice, Glory, Both now: the same. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Three regular Kathismata with their Sessional Hymns, then "And that we may be accounted worthy", Gospel of Matins[^570], according to the sequence of Gospels, Psalm 50 and "Save, O God"[^571]. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canon only one: triodion on Monday and Wednesday, or diodion [two-ode canon] on Tuesday. Troparia - on 12, heirmoi - twice. We do not recite the [Biblical] Odes, but only the refrain to the troparia of the canon "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee", then the Katavasia, heirmos of the canon once both choirs together. Before the Kontakion-Ikos the Priest sings the Small Litany; at the 9th Ode we do not sing "More honorable", nor "It is truly meet" after the canon, but immediately after the Katavasia - Small Litany and Exaposteilarion "Thy bridal chamber" twice, Glory, Both now: three times the same. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 4.	Stichera of the Praises - 4: of the Triodion, Glory, Both now: Triodion, Small Doxology and, after "Let us complete", - Aposticha of the Triodion and everything else with prostrations, as on the Lenten days of Great Lent. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H4: #### AT THE HOURS (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 1.	At the 1st and 9th Hours there will be no Kathisma, but it will be only at the 3rd and 6th. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Immediately after the Theotokion "O Theotokos, Thou Art the vine" at the 3rd Hour and, after the corresponding Theotokia, at the other two Hours, the rubrics prescribe the reading of the Four Gospels, which today among us is read only in cathedral, or conciliar, churches[^573]. At the 6th Hour we read the Gospel not immediately after the Theotokion, but after the conclusion of the Troparion of the Prophecy and the Paremia with its Prokimena. The reading of the Four Gospels covers all the Evangelists, that is Matthew, Mark and Luke in full, and John - only up to the "Passion of Christ", that is up to "Now is the Son of man glorified", Section 46, verse 13.32. All the Evangelists, taken together, are divided into 9 readings, according to the number of the three Hours (3, 6, 9) and three days (Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday). Matthew is divided into 2 readings, Mark - into 2, John - into 2, and Luke - into 3. The first reading covers the first half of Matthew, the second - the second half of Matthew, the third - the first half of Mark, the fourth - the second half of Mark, the fifth - the first part of Luke, the sixth - the second part of Luke, the seventh - the third part of Luke, the eighth - the first half of John, the ninth - the second half of John. The rubric does not indicate where the first half of the three Evangelists and the first and second parts of Luke end. But since Matthew has 28 chapters, its half ends with the 14th chapter, Mark - 16 chapters, so its half ends with the 8th chapter, Luke - 24 chapters, so its first part ends with the 8th chapter, and the second - with the 16th. John, up to "Now is [the Son of man] glorified", has 12 chapters and part of the 13th chapter, therefore its half ends with the 6th chapter and part of the 7th. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 3.	After the Trisagion - Kontakion of the Triodion. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 1.	Only one Kontakion of the Triodion. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	In Slavonic rubrics and the Greek Typikon of Sabbas, on Great Wednesday, instead of the dismissal the prayer "O Master most merciful" is given, which is at Vespers with All-Night Vigil, and in Slavonic ones - also the prayer of forgiveness for sins committed in Great Lent, and commemorations. However, these superfluous additions of the Greco-Slavs are unknown to the Greeks. The prayer "O Master most merciful" and the forgiveness may be retained perhaps only in monasteries. The commemorations, which are superfluous here, will be taken later, at the end of Compline and the Midnight Office, which have nothing in common with forgiveness for the sins of the Forty Days. (under ### 4.2.3 Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Instead of "God is the Lord" we sing Alleluia in Tone 8, that is in the tone of the following troparion; but since this troparion has a specific melody in the Heirmologion, we sing Alleluia according to the pattern of its melody. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Troparion "When the glorious disciples" twice, Glory, Both now: once more the same. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 4.	There will be no Kathismata and Sessional Hymns, but immediately the Gospel from Luke, Section 108 and 1st verse of Section 109, that is the 39th verse of the 22nd chapter[^577]; then Psalm 50 and, leaving out "Save, O God", we sing immediately the Canon. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 5.	Canon only one: heirmoi - twice, troparia - on 6[^578] with the refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee". Katavasia after each ode - heirmos of the same canon once, both choirs together. After the 3rd Ode we recite the Sessional Hymn, after the 6th - Kontakion and Ikos, at the 9th we do not sing "More honorable", nor after the 9th - "It is truly meet", but instead of this, - the heirmos of the canon[^580] and immediately the Exaposteilarion twice, Glory, Both now: the same. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 6.	Stichera of the Praises - 4 and, after the Small Doxology, - "Let us complete" and Aposticha with specific verses. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 8.	Priest: "Wisdom"; Choir: "Bless". (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H4: #### AT THE HOURS (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 1.	At all: Troparion and Kontakion of Great Thursday, however, at the 1st Hour, after the Theotokion "What shall we call Thee", we read the Troparion of the Prophecy, Prokimenon, Paremia and 2nd Prokimenon. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 2.	The Dismissal "Christ the Lord, Who goes"[^581] we recite at the 1st Hour, and at the others, since they are sung together with the Typika, we recite this dismissal at the end of the Typika. All this we recite in our cells. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 1.	Begins from the Beatitudes, without stichera. The Creed may be omitted, since it will be at the Liturgy. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Instead of the usual Kontakia we recite the Kontakion of Great Thursday. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 3.	After the Kontakion - "Lord, have mercy" (40), "O All-holy Trinity"[^582] and dismissal. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 1.	From the beginning to "Lord, I have cried" everything - according to the rubric of the Eve of the Nativity of Christ, here on pp. 169-170 [→REF:p169-170]. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 2.	On "Lord, I have cried" - stichera on 10. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Entrance with the Gospel and 3 readings of the Triodion, the two first - with their Prokimena, and the third - without Prokimenon. The Prokimenon of the day from the Horologion is not taken. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 4.	Immediately after the 3rd reading - Small Litany with the exclamation of the Trisagion, Trisagion, Prokimenon of the Triodion, Epistle and Gospel and, according to the order, Liturgy of Basil the Great. (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 5.	Instead of the Cherubic Hymn, also after the transfer of the Holy Gifts, instead of the Communion Hymn and "Let our mouths be filled", we sing the Troparion "Of Thy Mystical Supper". So in general editions. In our Manuscript Typikon, our Sluzhebnyky (lit. "Service Books") and the Moscow Typikon, instead of "In Thee rejoiceth", is prescribed the Heirmos of the 9th Ode of the Canon ("Come, let us the faithful"). The Euchologion of Benedict gives "Of Thy Mystical Supper" only instead of "In Thee rejoiceth". (In the order of the Liturgy of Basil). (under ### 4.2.4 Great Thursday)
  H5: ##### 1.	We sing this Matins after sunset; according to the rubric - at the 2nd hour of the night. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Before the holy doors is placed an analogion with two candlesticks, facing the people, and on it - the Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 1.	From the usual beginning to the end of the litany "In peace let us pray to the Lord" everything - as at Matins daily[^585]. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Instead of "God is the Lord" we sing Alleluia in the tone of the following troparion. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Troparion "When the glorious disciples" twice, Glory, Both now: once more the same. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 4.	When the troparion is sung the third time, the Priest vests in a violet phelonion, goes before the steps of the altar, bows to the Holy Mysteries and, having opened the holy doors, recites the prayer of the censer, after which, having blessed the censer: "Blessed is our God", censes around the holy table, then, having stood before the steps of the altar and bowed low, goes out through the holy doors before the iconostasis and censes the local icons, the choirs and the people and, having returned through the holy doors to the sanctuary, gives up the censer and, having again bowed low, sings the Small Litany without exclamation, then goes out through the holy doors to the analogion and exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy", "Peace be to all", "Wisdom, Aright" and the rest. The Choir, instead of "Glory to Thee, O Lord", sings "Glory to Thy Passion, O Lord"[^586]. Priest: "Let us attend" and reads the first Gospel. After the conclusion of the reading the Choir, instead of "Glory to Thee, O Lord", sings: "Glory to Thy long-suffering, O Lord"[^587]. The Priest, having kissed the beginning of the reading, returns to the sanctuary and, having closed the holy doors and bowed low to the Holy Mysteries, goes to the sacristy, takes off the phelonion and goes to his place. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the singing of the first Gospel follow five Sessional Hymns, each of which has before it three antiphons of the Passion troparia, and after itself - one Passion Gospel. The number of troparia in all antiphons is not the same and therefore, according to the rubric, each must be repeated[^590], except the Theotokion, with which each antiphon concludes, with the refrain "Glory, Both now"[^591]. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 6.	The Beatitudes, with the Prokimenon and the 6th Gospel at the end. The number of stichera on the Beatitudes - 7, and with "Glory, Both now" - 9[^592]. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 7.	Psalm 50 and the eighth Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 8.	Canon - triodion, heirmoi - twice, troparia - on 12, Katavasia after each ode - its heirmos, both choirs together. After the 5th Ode - Small Litany and Kontakion-Ikos; at the 9th Ode we do not sing "More honorable", but only the heirmos "More honorable"[^593]; after the 9th we do not sing "It is truly meet", but we sing the Katavasia[^594]; then - Small Litany and three times the Exaposteilarion with the refrain "Glory, Both now", as usual, then - the ninth Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 9.	Stichera of the Praises on 4, Glory: one sticheron, Both now: another, then - the tenth Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 11.	Litany "Let us complete" and, after the exclamation "For Thou Art a merciful [God]"[^596], immediately - the eleventh Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 12.	Aposticha and the twelfth Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 14.	Litany "Have mercy on us, O God" and the specific dismissal of Great Friday, intermediate, that is Priest: "Wisdom", Choir: "More honorable"; Priest: "Glory to Thee, O Christ" and the rest, as usual[^597]. We do not combine with the 1st Hour. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 7.	Priest: "Glory to Thee, O Christ God" and dismissal of Great Friday, to the people. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 1.	At this Vespers there will be an Entrance with the Gospel and, at its end, at the last sticheron of the Aposticha, - the procession with the Shroud and the placing of it in the Tomb[^600]. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 2.	All actions that the sacred ministers at usual Vespers perform before the iconostasis, at this Vespers they perform before the Tomb of God. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 1.	From the usual beginning to "Lord, I have cried" everything - as at Daily Vespers, without Kathisma. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 2.	On "Lord, I have cried" - stichera on 6, Glory: one sticheron, Both now: another. Entrance with the Gospel. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Instead of the Prokimenon of the day there will be Prokimenon of the Triodion, Paremia and third Paremia; then - third Prokimenon of the Apostle, Apostle, "Alleluia" and Gospel. At "Alleluia" there will be censing. "Let us attend. Peace be to all. Wisdom, let us attend" we exclaim only at the 1st and 3rd Prokimena. We begin the Gospel from "Wisdom, Aright". Instead of "Glory to Thee, O Lord" before the Gospel will be "Glory to Thy Passion, O Lord", and after the Gospel - "Glory to Thy long-suffering, O Lord", exactly so as at Matins. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 5.	At the last sticheron of the Aposticha, that is at "Thee, Who Art clothed with light", takes place the procession with the Shroud and the placing of it in the Tomb. According to the custom of our cathedral churches this takes place in the following way: During the singing of the Aposticha the procession is formed in the church. On the altar, on the left side, stands the monstrance, candles are lit on the altar and at the Tomb. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 1.	Where there will be only one priest, let him choose for himself four most honorable laymen to carry the Shroud. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 2.	If the pastor has several churches, then in the mother [church] He sings Vespers with the bringing out of the Shroud, and in the filial ones - only brings out the Shroud thus: after "Blessed is our God" and "O come, let us worship" (3) immediately - procession with "Thee, Who Art clothed with light" and, after the procession, - "Glory to Thee, O Christ God", dismissal and "The noble Joseph" (3) with prostrations. (under ### 4.2.5 Great Friday)
  H5: ##### 1.	This Matins we sing usually on Friday evening, after sunset, although the rubrics prescribe it at the 7th hour of the night. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	The sacred ministers remain during Matins before the Tomb of God, where seats and a table with candlesticks and books are prepared for them. Sitting, they sing Matins from there: the priests - in epitrachelia and phelonia, and the deacons - in dalmatics of violet color. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 1.	From "Blessed is our God" to "O come, let us worship" exclusively everything - as usual at Matins; then after "Lord, have mercy" (12), without "O come, let us worship" the Priest immediately: "Glory to the Holy" and the Six Psalms up to "God is the Lord", as usual[^603]. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	On "God is the Lord" - Troparion "The noble Joseph" (2), Glory: "When Thou didst descend", Both now: "The angel stood by the tomb"[^604]. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 4.	After "The Choir of angels" with the Small Litany - Sessional Hymn, 50th Psalm and Canon. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 5.	Canon: heirmoi - twice, troparia - on 12. Refrain - "Glory to Thee, O Lord, for Thy holy burial". Katavasia, heirmos of the canon, - both choirs together. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 7.	At the Great Doxology we make the Entrance with the Gospel and, instead of the one troparion after the Doxology, there will be one Sticheron of the Praises (Shroud [Epitaphios]), two troparia and three readings, that is one - of the Prophecy, one - of the Apostle and one - of the Gospel. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 8.	After the dismissal we sing the Troparion "The noble Joseph" (3) with prostrations exactly as at Vespers. The Deacon takes the Gospel from the analogion, the sacred ministers depart to the sanctuary and take off the sacred vestments, the Deacon places the Gospel on the altar and, having bowed, departs and unvests. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H4: #### AT THE HOURS (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 1.	At all: Troparion and Kontakion of Great Saturday. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	Dismissal of Matins of Great Saturday. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 1.	According to the custom of the archcathedral church, this Vespers begins at 9 o'clock in the day[^608]. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	On this day there will be only one Vespers, that is Great with the Liturgy of St. Basil and without All-Night Vigil, and after the Prayer behind the Ambo, or (as the rubrics prescribe) after the dismissal of the Liturgy, there will be the Blessing of Loaves, as usual at the All-Night Vigil. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 3.	The form and rubric of this Vespers is the same as the Vespers on the Eve of the Nativity, here on pp. 169-171 [→REF:p169-171], without "Blessed is the man"; only here, after "O Gladsome Light", there will be no Prokimenon, but immediately 15 readings, between which there will be no troparia, and at the 6th reading all verses of the Song of Moses are read with the refrain "For gloriously has He been glorified", and at the 15th reading the verses of the Song of the Three Youths are read with the refrain "Praise the Lord and exalt Him above all for ever". Instead of "Alleluia" we sing the last verse of the 81st Psalm with its other verses, in the form of a Prokimenon[^609]. Dismissal of Matins of Saturday. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 4.	Although the service of the feast usually begins at Vespers of the preceding day and therefore the whole Resurrection service should be celebrated in bright vestments, nevertheless, since the Liturgy, with which Vespers is sung, is still of the Saturday day, in the part of the verses on "Lord, I have cried" we sing about the descent of the Savior's soul into hell and therefore from the beginning of Vespers we use violet vestments, and afterwards change them after the conclusion of the Apostle, at the verses instead of "Alleluia", in order to read the Gospel already in bright vestments[^610]. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 5.	Throughout the whole Bright Week, beginning from the Vespers of this day, the Resurrection service is sung and, besides the stichera of the Triodion, are sung also the Resurrection stichera of the Octoechos, each day - in a different tone, that is on Sunday - in Tone 1, on Monday - in Tone 2, on Tuesday - in Tone 3, on Wednesday - in Tone 4, on Thursday - in Tone 5, on Friday - in Tone 6, on Saturday - in Tone 8, omitting Tone 7. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H4: #### AT COMPLINE (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 1.	From the usual beginning to "O come, let us worship" we celebrate everything, as usual at the Midnight Office; and immediately Psalm 50 and Canon. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 2.	The Tomb Canon of Matins of Great Saturday "He Who in ancient times hid the pursuing tyrant beneath the waves of the sea"; according to the rubric, heirmoi - twice, and troparia - on 12 (today - with heirmos on 4), with the refrain "Glory to Thee, O Lord, for Thy holy burial", after which Katavasia and heirmos - both choirs together. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 3.	Trisagion and the rest with "Our Father" and Troparion "When Thou didst descend". (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 4.	Litany "Have mercy on us, O God", which is at the Midnight Office, and dismissal of Matins of Great Saturday[^612], beginning from "Glory to Thee, O Christ God". (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 5.	At the end: "The noble Joseph" (3) with prostrations, as at Matins of Great Saturday, but the Shroud is kissed only by the sacred ministers, according to rank, and the Bishop, taking the Shroud from the Tomb, hands it to four canons and thus all the sacred ministers return to the sanctuary and four canons hang the Shroud behind the holy table over the High Place[^613]. Then they depart to the sacristy, take off the violet vestments and vest in all bright sacred vestments of their rank, and at this time in the church all candles are lit. (under ### 4.2.6 Matins of Great Saturday)
  H5: ##### 1.	PROCESSION BEFORE THE CHURCH DOORS. All people go out of the church, and after them go out also the sacred ministers. And the procession takes place thus: All sacred ministers, in all their sacred bright vestments, stand before the steps of the altar. The main sacred minister takes the cross, the Deacon - the censer, all together bow low to the Holy Mysteries and turn to the west. The main one begins to sing the sticheron "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior", all take up the singing of the sticheron and go out from the sanctuary to the church: the Priest - through the holy [doors], and all other sacred ministers - through the Deacon's doors; at this time all bells ring, and all go to the great church doors and, having gone out of the church, stand according to rank before the church doors, which are immediately closed[^615]. In the middle of the church is placed on the holy table first of all the icon of the Resurrection of Christ, usually on the Artos, also other holy icons, reliquaries and the book of the Holy Gospels in the most precious binding or something else of the sacred things, all this - according to the number of priests who will give them to be kissed by the people at the end of Matins. Outside, before the church doors, Matins begins in the following way[^616]. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 2.	BEGINNING OF MATINS. Immediately after the church doors are closed, the main priest, holding with his left hand the cross, and with his right - the censer and censing the church doors[^617], exclaims loudly: "Glory to the Holy" and the rest. The Choir responds: "Amen"; the Priest gives up the censer and sings loudly the troparion "Christ is risen", also all - the same, then the Priest the second time - the same and all - the same, and the third time the Priest - the same and all - the same, then the Priest in a high voice exclaims the verses, and all after each verse sing this troparion once[^618]. At the end the Priest, having sung in a higher voice the same troparion only up to "trampling down death by death" inclusive, opens the doors, and all finish: "and upon those in the tombs bestowing life" and again all bells ring, all enter the church[^619]. The Matins prayers at Resurrection Matins throughout the whole Bright Week are not recited, for they are connected with the Six Psalms, and since there will be no Six Psalms here, there will be no prayers. If it were necessary to recite the prayers, it should be indicated in the rubric where they should be recited, for during the verses "Let God arise", which take the place of the Six Psalms, it is not possible to recite them, for at this time the rubric orders the Priest to sing verses, and not to add prayers as well. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 3.	ENTRANCE INTO THE CHURCH. The entrance of the procession into the church takes place in the reverse order to the exit from the church, that is first enters the main priest, after him - other priests and sacred ministers, all the clergy, then singers, brotherhood, banner- and icon-bearers, the cross-bearer and all people. The priests enter the sanctuary and approach the holy table. The Bishop, if there be one, departs to his throne. The Deacon stands in his place before the holy doors and sings from there the litany. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 4.	After the entrance into the church the first deacon sings the Great Litany "In peace let us pray to the Lord", standing in the usual place, and immediately we sing the Canon. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 5.	Canon of the Resurrection: heirmoi - 4 times (in Greek ones - twice), troparia - on 12. (Today heirmoi - once, troparia with heirmos - on 4). Katavasia after each ode - heirmos of the canon, both choirs together - once. The Priest sings the heirmoi, and the choirs - the troparia and Katavasia. Refrain to the troparia of the canon - "Christ is risen from the dead", at the penultimate - "Glory", and at the last - "Both now", as usual. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 6.	Stichera of the Praises - 8: 4 Sunday of the Octoechos of Tone 1, with the usual verses from "Praise Him", and 4 of the Resurrection with specific verses, then Glory, Both now: to the Resurrection, "Christ is risen" (3), can be more, until the kissing is finished. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 7.	KISSING ONE ANOTHER. Is prescribed in the rubrics at "Christ is risen", after the conclusion of the last sticheron of the Resurrection. However, today we do not kiss one another as in the sticheron: "and let us embrace one another", but we kiss only the precious cross, the Gospel and holy icons, which the priests hold, greeting everyone who approaches with the words: "Christ is risen", and they answer: "Truly He is risen". If there be many people, then we begin the kissing not after the stichera of the Resurrection, but from their beginning, that is from the verse "Let God arise". If there be very many people, then the kissing will be also during the canon. And this kissing takes place thus: (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 8.	Litanies "Have mercy on us, O God" and "Let us complete" are sung by deacons, standing in their usual places. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 9.	Great Dismissal, specific, at which, instead of "More honorable", we sing "Shine, shine"[^638] and, instead of "Glory, Both now", we sing "Christ is risen" once. The Priest stands with the cross in the holy doors, and the Deacon (if there be none, then the Priest himself) exclaims: "Wisdom". Choir (or second deacon, if there be one)[^623]: "Bless". (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H4: #### AT THE LITURGY (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 1.	Instead of "O Heavenly King", at the Proskomedia, at the Liturgy and wherever else, we recite "Christ is risen" (3) until the Ascension; and from the Ascension to the Descent [of the Holy Spirit], instead of "O Heavenly King", we read nothing[^625]. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 2.	Regarding "Christ is risen", one should know that at "Glory to Thee, O Christ God" before the dismissal of the Proskomedia and at the Liturgy we recite "Christ is risen" once, "Lord, have mercy" (3) and "Lord, bless". (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 3.	Instead of the Trisagion we sing "All Who have been baptized into Christ". (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 4.	During the reading of the Gospel, at each verse, all bells are rung[^627]. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 5.	Instead of "It is truly meet", according to the new rubric, we sing the heirmos "Shine, shine" with the refrain "The angel cried" as in the Triodion and in the Constantinopolitan Typikon, but without the troparia of the 9th Ode of the Canon, as in our Sluzhebnyky, for these troparia are not about the Most Holy Theotokos, but about Christ's Resurrection. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 6.	Before the dismissal, instead of "More honorable", we sing "Shine, shine" and instead of "Glory, Both now" - "Christ is risen" once. Dismissal with the cross and the rest, which is after the dismissal with the raising of the cross exactly as was indicated for Resurrection Matins. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 7.	BLESSING OF THE ARTOS. After the Prayer behind the Ambo[^629] takes place the blessing of the Artos, for which you have the prayer in the Sluzhebnik after the Liturgy of the Resurrection; after the prayer the Artos is sprinkled with holy water. And this Artos with the feast, that is the icon of the Resurrection, after the dismissal of the Liturgy, is carried from the altar, on which it was blessed, to the tetrapod, where it remains until Bright Saturday and the faithful entering the church kiss it. On Bright Saturday at the Liturgy, after the Prayer behind the Ambo, the Priest, having come out through the holy doors, reads over it the prayer for breaking, given only in our Sluzhebnik on Bright Saturday, and after this, it is taken from the tetrapod, broken in the sacristy and on the next day, that is on the Sunday of Ap. Thomas, after the Prayer behind the Ambo, is distributed to the faithful by the Priest standing in the holy doors[^630]. (under ### 4.2.8 Resurrection Matins)
  H5: ##### 1.	The sacred ministers, dressed in all sacred vestments of their rank during the whole Vespers stand in the sanctuary at the altar, coming out only for the Entrance (and deacons - also for the litanies) before the holy doors and for the final kissing of the sacred insignia, if there be several priests - as was given for Matins[^631]. (under ### 4.2.9 On the Day of Resurrection in the Evening)
  H5: ##### 2.	Censing is done three times, that is at the usual beginning, only before the holy table, then at "Lord, I have cried" and at "O Gladsome Light", as usual. (under ### 4.2.9 On the Day of Resurrection in the Evening)
  H5: ##### 3.	Entrance takes place with the Gospel, for it is to be read. (under ### 4.2.9 On the Day of Resurrection in the Evening)
  H5: ##### 1.	The Priest and deacons, if there be any, dressed in all sacred vestments of their rank, come before the steps of the altar and bow low, as usual. The Priest receives the censer with the usual prayer of blessing the censer, censes before the holy table, exclaiming: "Blessed is our God"[^632]; then, having given up the censer, ascends with the deacons the steps and sings "Christ is risen" once and both choirs - once each. After this the Priest recites clearly "Let God arise" and other verses with "Glory, Both now". The choirs, at each verse, sing "Christ is risen" once or three times exactly as was indicated for the beginning of Matins. Afterwards the Priest: "Christ is risen" up to "trampling down death by death", and the choirs: "and upon those in the tombs" and the rest. (under ### 4.2.9 On the Day of Resurrection in the Evening)
  H5: ##### 4.	Entrance with the Gospel and usual censing at "O Gladsome Light"; and, after the Great Prokimenon, the Deacon, and if there be none, then the Priest himself: "And that we may be accounted worthy", priest: "The Reading from John...". Deacon: "Let us attend", and the Priest himself reads the Gospel from the holy doors (From John Sect. 65, up to "not believe"). (under ### 4.2.9 On the Day of Resurrection in the Evening)
  H5: ##### 6.	At the Aposticha: one Resurrection Sticheron of the Octoechos, given in the Triodion, then Resurrection Stichera with their verses and the kissing of the cross, or also of other sacred insignia exactly as was given for Matins. After the final "Christ is risen" immediately dismissal with the cross and the rest exactly as was given for Matins. (under ### 4.2.9 On the Day of Resurrection in the Evening)
  H5: ##### 1.	At the beginning of Matins we do not go out before the church doors, but begin it in the sanctuary, before the altar[^634]. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 2.	We sing the Small Litany only after the 3rd, 6th and 9th Odes. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H4: #### AT THE HOURS (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H4: #### AT THE LITURGY (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 1.	Everything - as on the day of Resurrection, changing daily the Prokimenon, Apostle, Alleluia and Gospel. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 2.	On Saturday, after the Prayer behind the Ambo, we read the prayer for the breaking of the Artos, given in our Liturgikon, in the order of Bright Saturday[^635]. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 1.	At the beginning of every service, instead of "O Heavenly King", we sing "Christ is risen" (3)[^636], and at the end of every divine service, according to our custom, we also sing "Christ is risen" (3), also at the end we add: "and to us He has granted life eternal, let us worship His Resurrection on the third day"[^637]; and before the dismissal, instead of "More honorable", we sing "Shine, shine"[^638]. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 2.	At Vespers: On Sunday evening for Monday there will be no Entrance, as happens on the Sundays of Lent (for here there is no Great Prokimenon, and the Triodia give the litany "Have mercy on us, O God" at the end of Vespers), except the Sunday of Ap. Thomas, which, as a feast of the Lord, has also a Great Prokimenon. Finally, in the provinces the Great Entrance is made every Sunday evening, regardless of whether there is a feast or not. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 3.	At Compline: From today we begin to sing the Canons of the Theotokos. After "It is truly meet" throughout the whole week, we recite the Kontakion of the Sunday; only during the eight days of the feast of Mid-Pentecost we read the Kontakion of Mid-Pentecost. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 4.	We sing the Midnight Office as usual, only after the 1st Trisagion on the feast of Mid-Pentecost[^642] we take the Troparion of Mid-Pentecost, and on the Apodosis of the Resurrection[^643] - the Resurrection Troparion of the Sunday with its Theotokion; and after the 2nd Trisagion in the middle of Mid-Pentecost we take the Kontakion of Mid-Pentecost, and outside Mid-Pentecost - Kontakion of the Resurrection. The prayer "Remember, O Lord" will not be. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 5.	We begin Matins from "Glory to the Holy" and immediately "Christ is risen" (3), then "Glory to God in the highest" and the rest; before the 50th Psalm we recite "The Resurrection of Christ": on Sundays - three times, and on weekdays - once[^639]. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 6.	At the Hours: after "Blessed is our God" we recite "Christ is risen" (3), and after the dismissal - "Christ is risen" (3) and add: "and to us He has granted life eternal" and further. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 7.	At the Liturgy: "Christ is risen" we sing where also on the Resurrection of Christ; here, on pp. 416-418 [→REF:p416-418]. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H5: ##### 8.	The services of saints without Polyeleos, which fall on these Sundays, as also on others until Pentecost, on the feast of Mid-Pentecost and on the Apodosis of the Resurrection, we take at Compline. (under ### 4.2.10 General Rubric for All Days of Bright Week)
  H4: #### AT Great Vespers (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)
  H5: ##### 2.	On "Lord, I have cried" - 10 stichera: 4 Sunday and 6 of the Myrrh-bearers (3 which are at "Lord, I have cried", and 3 which are at the Litiya); Glory: of the Myrrh-bearers, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the tone (Dogmatikon). (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)
  H5: ##### 3.	At the Aposticha - Stichera of the Resurrection with their verses; Glory: "Thee, Who Art clothed with light", Both now: "The Day of Resurrection"[^644]. (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)
  H5: ##### 4.	After the final Trisagion: Sunday Troparion of Tone 2; Glory: "The noble Joseph", Both now: "The angel stood by the tomb". (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)
  H5: ##### 5.	Dismissal of the Resurrection, without the cross. (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)

  H5: ##### 2.	After the 1st and 2nd Stichology - Sessional Hymns of the Myrrh-bearers; after "The Blameless" - "The Choir of angels"; Hypakoe of the tone and everything else up to the canon - Sunday, as usual. Gospel will be the 2nd Sunday and "The Resurrection of Christ" three times (according to others - once). (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canons 3 [making] 14: Resurrection with heirmos on 4, Theotokos of the Triodion on 2 and of the Myrrh-bearers on 8. Katavasia of the Resurrection. (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)
  H5: ##### 4.	From the Praises to the end everything - according to the general rubric of the Sunday. (under ### 4.2.12 Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women)

  H5: ##### 4.	At all Hours: Troparion of Mid-Pentecost, and at the 3rd and 9th - Glory: of the saint; Kontakia: at the 1st and at the 6th - to Mid-Pentecost, and at the 3rd and 9th - to the saint. (under ### 4.2.14 Mid-Pentecost)
  H5: ##### 5.	At the Liturgy: Troparion, Glory, Both now: Kontakion of Mid-Pentecost. (under ### 4.2.14 Mid-Pentecost)

  H5: ##### 1.	Antiphons, as every Sunday. (under ### 4.2.15 Sunday of the Samaritan Woman)

  H5: ##### 1.	On "Lord, I have cried" - stichera of the Blind Man on 6, Glory, Both now: His. (under ### 4.2.16 Sunday of the Man Born Blind)

  H5: ##### 1.	On "God is the Lord": Sunday Troparion twice, Glory, Both now: its Theotokion. (under ### 4.2.16 Sunday of the Man Born Blind)

  H5: ##### 3.	Canons 3 [making] 14: Resurrection with heirmos on 6, of the Blind Man on 4 and of the Forefeast of the Ascension on 4. Katavasia of the Ascension. (under ### 4.2.16 Sunday of the Man Born Blind)

After the 3rd Ode - Kontakion-Ikos of the Blind Man and his Sessional Hymn, Glory, Both now: of the Forefeast; after the 6th - Kontakion-Ikos of the Resurrection; at the 9th, instead of "More honorable", we sing "The angel cried" and "Shine, shine"; after the 9th - Exaposteilarion of the Resurrection twice, Glory, Both now: of the Blind Man. (under ### 4.2.16 Sunday of the Man Born Blind)
  H5: ##### 5.	Stichera of the Praises on 8: of the Blind Man on 4 and Stichera of the Resurrection with their verses; Glory, Both now: "The Day of Resurrection" and "Christ is risen" once. (under ### 4.2.16 Sunday of the Man Born Blind)
  H5: ##### 6.	After the Great Doxology - Troparion "Let us the faithful praise", Glory, Both now: its Theotokion; after the litanies - Great Dismissal, of Pascha, without the cross, with the commemoration of the saint of the day. (under ### 4.2.16 Sunday of the Man Born Blind)

  H4: #### AT Great Vespers (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "Lord, I have cried" - 10 stichera: 3 Sunday, 3 of the Ascension and 4 of the Fathers; Glory: of the Fathers, Both now: 1st Theotokion. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)

  H5: ##### 1.	On "God is the Lord": Sunday Troparion twice, Glory, Both now: of the feast. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 2.	Usual Kathismata and everything else up to the canon - Sunday, as usual, only the Gospel will be the 10th Sunday. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canons 3 [making] 14: Sunday with heirmos on 4, Ascension on 4 and of the Fathers on 6. Katavasia of the Descent. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 4.	Stichera of the Praises on 8: 4 Sunday and of the Fathers on 4; Glory: of the Fathers, Both now: "Most blessed Art Thou". (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the Great Doxology - Sunday Troparion, one of the two usual, and after the dismissal - Glory, Both now: 6th Gospel Sticheron. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H4: #### AT THE HOURS (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H4: #### AT THE LITURGY (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 1.	After the Entrance: Sunday Troparion, of the feast and of the Fathers; Glory: Kontakion to the Fathers, Both now: of the feast. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 2.	Everything else - only to the Fathers, only Communion Hymn - "Praise the Lord" and to the Fathers ("Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous"). (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "Lord, I have cried" - 6 stichera: 3 of the Fathers and 3 of the saint of the Menaion, Glory: of the Fathers, Both now: of the feast. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 2.	At the Aposticha - Stichera of the Fathers, only Both now: of the feast. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 3.	After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart" - Troparion of the Fathers, Glory: of the saint, Both now: of the feast. And thus the service of the Holy Fathers is leavetaken. (under ### 4.2.18 Sunday of the Holy Fathers of the First Nicaean Council)
  H5: ##### 1.	The service of this feast is celebrated for seven days, for it is leavetaken on the following Saturday. And it has this peculiarity, that on Sunday evening at Vespers for Monday prayers with bending of knees are read, and in the morning, that is on Monday the whole service of the feast is sung, without the Menaion, and at the Liturgy - also the service of the Holy Trinity, if desired[^654], as we will note immediately. Everything else, from the feast to its Apodosis, is celebrated according to the general rule of the feast, in the middle of a feast of the Lord. At the Liturgy "All Who have been baptized into Christ" - only on the feast itself. (under ### 4.2.19 Sunday of Pentecost)
  H5: ##### 2.	Here they have the custom on the day of Pentecost itself at the beginning of the Liturgy, after "Blessed is the Kingdom" (better before "Blessed is the Kingdom"), and afterwards, at the beginning of Vespers, after "Blessed is our God", to sing "O Heavenly King", bending the knees. (under ### 4.2.19 Sunday of Pentecost)
  H5: ##### 3.	Vespers on the day of the feast itself we sing immediately after the Liturgy, in view of the prayers with bending of knees, so that all people may participate in them[^655]. (under ### 4.2.19 Sunday of Pentecost)
  H5: ##### 4.	The service of a saint who falls on Sunday and on Monday, we sing on another day at Compline, by the decision of the Rector of the church. (under ### 4.2.19 Sunday of Pentecost)
  H5: ##### 5.	Throughout this week at Compline - Canon of the Most Holy Theotokos, and also it is permitted to eat meat. (under ### 4.2.19 Sunday of Pentecost)
  H5: ##### 1.	Immediately after the Liturgy the Priest and deacon, if there be one, dressed in all sacred vestments, standing at the altar, begin Vespers. Priest: "Blessed is our God" and, after "Amen", all, bending our knees, we sing "O Heavenly King", then we recite the Trisagion and the rest with "Our Father" and "O come, let us worship" (3), as usual. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 2.	Great Litany "In peace let us pray to the Lord" with the addition of specific petitions[^656]. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 3.	On "Lord, I have cried" - stichera of the feast on 6, Glory, Both now: "O Heavenly King". (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 4.	After the Entrance with the censer - Great Prokimenon of the feast. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the singing of the Prokimenon the Deacon or priest, standing in the sanctuary, begins the Small Litany thus: "Again and again, bending our knees, let us pray to the Lord"; choir: "Lord, have mercy" (3) and, when all bend their knees, the Priest, kneeling outside the sanctuary, before the holy doors[^657], reads aloud, so that all may hear, two prayers with bending of knees; after the conclusion of them all rise, and the Deacon continues the Small Litany, saying: "Help us, save us"; choir: "Lord, have mercy" once. Deacon: "Commemorating our most holy, most pure"; priest - specific exclamation. We do exactly so after "Let us all say" and after "Vouchsafe"[^658]. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 6.	Aposticha of the feast; after "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart" - Troparion of the feast and the Great Specific Dismissal of this Vespers. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H4: #### AT COMPLINE (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "God is the Lord": Troparion of the feast three times. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 2.	After the two usual Kathismata - Sessional Hymns of the feast and immediately Psalm 50 and Canon. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canons of the feast 2 [making] 14: first - with heirmos on 8, and second - with heirmos on 6. Katavasia of the feast. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 4.	Stichera of the Praises of the feast on 4; Glory, Both now: of the feast. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the Great Doxology - Troparion of the feast; after the litanies - Great Dismissal of the feast "Who sent down from heaven". (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H4: #### AT THE LITURGY (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 1.	Antiphons: two first - of the feast, third - daily with the refrain of the feast. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 2.	Entrance - specific; Troparion, Glory, Both now: Kontakion of the feast and Trisagion. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H5: ##### 3.	Prokimenon and Alleluia - specific; Apostle and Gospel - of the day; Communion Hymn - of the feast. (under ### 4.2.20 Monday of the Holy Spirit)
  H4: #### AT Great Vespers (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 2.	On "Lord, I have cried" - 10 stichera: 4 Sunday and 6 of the Saints; Glory: of the Saints, Both now: 1st Theotokion of the tone (8th). (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 3.	After the Great Prokimenon - 3 readings to the Saints. (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 4.	Sunday Aposticha; Glory: of the Saints, Both now: their Theotokion. (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 5.	After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart" - Sunday Troparion, Glory: of the Saints, Both now: Sunday Theotokion in the tone of the Saints. (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H4: #### AT Great Matins (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 1.	On "God is the Lord": Sunday Troparion twice, Glory: of the Saints, Both now: Sunday Theotokion in the tone of the Saints. (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 2.	Regular Kathismata and everything else up to the canon - Sunday, as usual. (Gospel - 1st regular). (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canons 4 [making] 14: Sunday on 4, Cross-Resurrection on 2, Theotokos on 2 and of the Saints on 6. Katavasia regular ("I shall open my mouth"). (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 4.	Stichera of the Praises - 8: 5 Sunday and 3 of the Saints with their two verses; Glory: 1st Gospel Sticheron, Both now: "Most blessed Art Thou". (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the Great Doxology - Sunday Troparion, one of the two usual; everything else - usual, as every Sunday. (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H4: #### AT THE LITURGY (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 1.	After the Entrance: Troparion Sunday and of the Saints; Glory, Both now: Kontakion of the Saints. (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H5: ##### 2.	Prokimenon Sunday of the tone and of the Saints; Alleluia of the Saints, Apostle and Gospel of the Saints; Communion Hymn "Praise the Lord" and of the Saints ("Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous"). (under ### 4.2.21 Sunday of All Saints)
  H4: #### AT THE LITURGY (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)
  H5: ##### 1.	If there be no monstrance, then the Holy Mysteries are exposed in the pyx. (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)
  H5: ##### 2.	After the cleansing of the chalice the Priest does not place the Gospel on the antimension, since after the Prayer behind the Ambo the Holy Mysteries will have to be placed on the altar. (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)
  H5: ##### 3.	If there be no deacons, then the Priest does all this himself. (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)
  H5: ##### 1.	In case it is impossible to have four altars, see what the Synod of Lviv prescribes here, on page 448. (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)
  H5: ##### 2.	The Lamb placed in the tabernacle, let the Priest consume on the next day at the Liturgy together with the other, consecrated at this Liturgy: first - the consecrated, and then - the presanctified. (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)
  H5: ##### 3.	On Sunday evening at Vespers we sing the Great Prokimenon of the feast, given in the Triodion on the previous Thursday evening. (under ### 4.2.23 Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist)

========================================
FILE: Final_Dolnytsky_part5_temple.md
========================================
  H5: ##### 2.	The feast of a temple saint, even if of a lower rank, exceeds even a feast of a saint with a Vigil, primarily in that, when it falls on a Sunday, it includes at Matins before the Canon the Prokimenon, the Gospel, and the Sunday Sticheron [after the Gospel], exactly as a Feast of the Theotokos; also, the Gospel Sticheron is transferred from the "Praises" to the end of Matins. Besides this, if it falls on a weekday, it excludes at the Liturgy the Apostle and Gospel of the day, which is then read on the day before the feast. Finally, the temple feast of a saint has its Apodosis, which takes place on the very day of the feast at Vespers for the following day, with an Entrance, as on a Feast of the Lord. In this Apodosis at "Lord, I have cried," there will be 6 stichera: 3 of the temple and 3 of the daily saint; Glory: of the temple; Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday [*Octoechos*] (Dogmatic) according to the tone of the Doxastikon. If the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, then also Glory, Both now: of the temple. On Friday evening for Saturday, we always sing the Dogmatic of the tone that is being given away. After the Prokimenon - "Let us all say." Aposticha of the temple with its verses, Glory: of the temple, Both now: Theotokion from the Sunday [*Octoechos*], according to the tone of the Doxastikon. If the temple is of the Lord or of the Theotokos, then Both now: of the temple. At the end: Troparion of the temple, Glory: Troparion of the daily saint, Glory, Both now: of the temple. Great Dismissal with the commemoration of the temple and the daily saint. At this Vespers, the service of the temple saint concludes; however, a temple saint does not have an Apodosis if the following day falls on a Sunday, or a Polyeleos saint. (under ### 5.2.1 General Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 3.	If the feast of a temple saint falls in the midst of a feast, that is, during the Forefeast, Afterfeast, or Apodosis of a feast - observe the rubric of a Saint with Vigil in the Afterfeast, whether on a Sunday or on a weekday here, on pp. 104-105 [→REF:p104-105][^667], only on Sunday, at Matins, we sing the Prokimenon, Gospel and Sticheron of the temple; at the Praises - Glory: of the temple, and the Gospel Sticheron - after the dismissal of Matins[^668]. (under ### 5.2.1 General Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 4.	On the very day of the feast, after the Ambo [prayer], in our lands, a Blessing of Water usually takes place outside the church, to where a procession goes: in front - the cross-bearer, then - the censer-bearer, the Deacon with the Gospel, and the Priest with a hand cross. It befits the church servers to carry there also the boat with incense, the sprinkler with the holy water vessel, a glass [cup], and a towel, so that the cross may be wiped after immersing it in the water, as well as the hands of the Priest. After the dismissal of the Liturgy, the Polychronion is sung for the Ecumenical Pontiff, the Emperor, the Metropolitan, one's own Bishop, all present sacred ministers, and all people of the parish. (under ### 5.2.1 General Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 5.	On the day following the feast, according to local custom, a Liturgy for the deceased of the parish with a Parastas is sung, at which it is not fitting to take anything of the feast, but everything only - for the deceased. (under ### 5.2.1 General Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 6.	The feast of a temple saint is usually transferred among us to the following Sunday; in throne [cathedral] temples - it is celebrated on its own day. (under ### 5.2.1 General Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 1.	At "Lord, I have cried": 4 stichera of the temple, Glory: of the same, Both now: of the Indiction. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 2.	At the Aposticha: stichera of the Indiction, Glory: of the temple, Both now: of the Indiction. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 3.	At the end: Troparion of the Indiction, Glory: of the temple, Both now: of the Synaxis of the Theotokos[^670]. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)

  H5: ##### 2.	At the Litiya: stichera of the temple, Glory: of the temple, Both now: Theotokion according to the tone of the Doxastikon[^691], from the Aposticha of Sunday Great Vespers. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 3.	At the Aposticha: stichera of the temple, Glory: of the same, Both now: of the Indiction. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)

From the beginning, that is from "Glory to the Holy," until the Polyeleos, everything is as without a temple here, on p. 111 [→REF:p111]; only after the second poetic Kathisma [stichologia], the Doxastikon of Kallistos is not taken and we sing Glory, Both now: Theotokion of the Indiction.   H5: ##### 1. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON THE SUNDAYS (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)

  H5: ##### 2.	AT Great Vespers: at the Litiya we sing the stichera of the *Triodion* from the Aposticha stichera, then - of the temple; Glory: of the temple, Both now: one of the Aposticha stichera of the *Triodion*; only on Meatfare and Cheesefare Sundays, since the Theotokion of the *Triodion* is specific, we conclude the Doxastikon with it.   H5: ##### 3.	AT Great Matins: Canons 3 on 14: Sunday on 4, *Triodion* on 4 and temple on 6. Katavasia of the season, only on Meatfare Sunday the Katavasia is of the *Triodion*. Stichera at the Praises - 8: 4 Sunday and 4 of the temple with its verses, Glory: of the temple, Both now: "Most Blessed"; however, on Meatfare and Cheesefare Sundays, after four stichera of the temple with their verses, we sing the Idiomelon sticheron of the *Triodion* with the verse "Arise, O Lord," Glory: the same, Both now: "Most Blessed"[^684][^678]. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)

  H4: #### AT Great Matins (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 1.	Troparia at "God is the Lord," at the end of Matins, at the Hours and at the Liturgy we sing according to the general rubric of a Polyeleos saint in the midst of a feast. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 2.	Two Kathismata with Sessional Hymns of Lazarus, then - Polyeleos and all three Sessional Hymns of the temple; everything else until the Canon is of the temple; only after the Gospel we say "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 3.	Canons 3 on 14: Lazarus the first with Heirmos on 6, temple on 4 and Lazarus the second on 4. Katavasia - Heirmos of the 2nd Canon of Lazarus. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 4.	Stichera at the Praises - 8: 4 of Lazarus and 4 of the temple with its verses, Glory: of the temple, Both now: of Lazarus. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 5.	After the Doxology - Troparion of the temple, Glory, Both now: of Lazarus. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 17. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON PALM SUNDAY (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 18. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON PASSION WEEK (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 19. TEMPLE OF A SAINT FROM BRIGHT MONDAY (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 20. TEMPLE OF A SAINT (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 21. TEMPLE OF A SAINT (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 22. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON THE THURSDAY OF ASCENSION (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 23. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON THE SEVENTH SUNDAY (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 24. TEMPLE OF A SAINT WITH THE APODOSIS OF ASCENSION (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 25. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON SOUL SATURDAY (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 26. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON THE VERY DAY OF PENTECOST (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 27. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON MONDAY OF THE HOLY SPIRIT (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 28. TEMPLE OF A SAINT (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 29. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON THE SUNDAY OF ALL SAINTS (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 30. TEMPLE OF A SAINT ON THE SUNDAY OF THE FEAST OF THE EUCHARIST (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 31. TEMPLE OF A SAINT (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 1. FROM THE PUBLICAN TO PASCHA (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 2. FROM PASCHA TO ALL SAINTS (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 1.	Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee, hearken and have mercy. Let us all say. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 2.	Again we pray for our Most Holy Ecumenical Pontiff (name) the Pope of Rome, for our God-loving and God-protected Emperor (name), for the state, victory, welfare, peace, health, salvation and for the remission of their sins and that the Lord God may help and especially assist them in everything and subdue under their feet every enemy and adversary. Let us all say. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 3.	Again we pray for our Most Reverend Archbishop Metropolitan Kyr (name), for our God-loving Bishop Kyr (name), for our spiritual fathers and for all our brethren in Christ, for health, salvation and for the remission of their sins. Let us all say. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 4.	Again we pray for every Christian soul: for health, salvation and remission of their sins. Let us all say. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 5.	Again we pray for all those who serve and have served in this holy temple: for health, salvation and for the remission of their sins. Let us all say. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 1.	When a service is transferred from a weekday to Sunday, the service of the saint falling on that Sunday is transferred to the weekday of the service being transferred to Sunday. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 2.	In the Menologion we will present the names only of those saints who have a service, others - no, for we do not have to mention them, but only the former. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 3.	Let the one who officiates not omit the services of feasts on 6 with Great Doxology and services of feasts of a lower rank compared to them. With the services of Polyeleos feasts and of a higher rank compared to them, let him not combine services for the deceased, but take only the service of the feast with the intention for the deceased[^696]. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 4.	Services of the whole year: some - immovable, that is of the *Menaion*, and others - movable, that is of the *Triodia* and *Octoechos*. We will present first the Rule of immovable services, and then - of movable[^697]. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H5: ##### 5.	Since the services of the *Menaion* are diverse, so that the Reader may know what kind of service he should take, to each service on the left side we add one of the signs determining the kind of service. (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H4: #### RULE OF IMMOVABLE SERVICES (under ### 5.2.2 Specific Temple Rubrics)
  H4: #### LENTEN TRIODION (ПІСНА ТРІОДЬ) (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. In the first five days, from Monday to Meatfare Friday, the *Triodion* has nothing, only the *Octoechos* and *Menaion*. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. Meatfare Saturday - Soul Saturday (Commemoration of the Dead). (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. On Monday, Tuesday and Thursday in the *Triodion* there is little; the rest is in the *Octoechos* and *Menaion*. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. On Wednesday and Friday the *Triodion* has a fuller Lenten service. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. In the five days, from Monday to Friday of the 1st week of Lent, the service is Lenten. On Monday **Great Lent** begins. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. First Saturday of Great Lent. Great Martyr Theodore the Tyro. -- - (under ### August (Серпень))
  H4: #### GREAT LENT (ВЕЛИКИЙ ПІСТ) (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. In the five days, from Monday to Friday of the 2nd week of Lent, the service is Lenten. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. From Monday onwards we take at Compline the canon of the saints of the *Menaion*, which fall from Lazarus Saturday to St. Thomas Sunday, both inclusive. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. Second Saturday of Lent: service to the martyrs, hierarchs and all saints, also to the dead - according to the rule of Saturdays with "Alleluia," with the service of the saint of the *Menaion*. (The same for the 3rd and 4th Saturdays of Lent). (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. In the five days, from Monday to Friday of the 3rd week of Lent, the service is Lenten. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. Third Saturday of Lent: service of Saturday with "Alleluia" just as on the 2nd Saturday of Lent. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. In the five days, from Monday to Friday of the 4th week of Lent, the service is of the Veneration of the Cross. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. Fourth Saturday of Lent: service - as on the 2nd and 3rd Saturdays of Lent. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. On Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday and Friday of the 5th week of Lent the service is Lenten. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. On Thursday of the 5th week of Lent: service of the Repentance of the Great Canon, with prostrations. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. Saturday of the 5th week of Lent - Akathist Saturday. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. On Great Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday the service is of the Passion, anticipating Christ's Passion[^746]. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. On Great Thursday: Mystical Supper and beginning of Christ's Passion. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. On Great Friday: Christ's Passion. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 4. On Great Saturday: Burial of Christ in the Tomb. -- - (under ### August (Серпень))
  H4: #### PASCHAL CYCLE (П'ЯТИДЕСЯТНИЦЯ) (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. From Monday to Bright Saturday inclusive we sing the whole service - The Resurrection. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. On Monday and Tuesday we abstain from work and participate in the divine service. During the week meat is permitted. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. Monday and Tuesday - in the midst of the feast of the Paralytic, with *Menaion*. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost. 8-day feast, without Vigil and without Polyeleos. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. Monday and Tuesday - in the midst of the feast of Mid-Pentecost; service of the Samaritan Woman is delayed until the Apodosis of the feast of Mid-Pentecost. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. Thursday, Friday and Saturday - in the midst of the feast of the Samaritan Woman. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday - in the midst of the feast of the Blind Man; on Wednesday - also **[GT DOX]**: Apodosis of the Resurrection and forefeast of Ascension. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. Thursday: **[LORD] = ASCENSION.** **9-day feast with Vigil.** (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday and Thursday - in the midst of the feast of Ascension. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. Friday: **[GT DOX]** Apodosis of Ascension. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. Soul Saturday. All - according to the rule of Meatfare Saturday here, on pg. 320. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. Monday of the Holy Spirit (and feast of the Most Holy Trinity - if desired). Abstinence from work and presence at divine service. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. During the week meat is permitted. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. Saturday: **[GT DOX]** Apodosis of the Descent of the Holy Spirit. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. In this Sunday begins the column of Sunday Matins Gospels and Liturgical days. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. On Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday the *Triodion* has nothing. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. Thursday: **[LORD] = EUCHARIST.** **8-day feast. Vigil is transferred to Sunday.** (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1. With this Sunday begins the column of Tones. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2. On Thursday: **[GT DOX]** Apodosis of the feast of the Eucharist. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3. On Friday: **[POL]** **Co-Suffering of the Most Holy Theotokos.** One-day feast, polyeleos. *On this day ends the New *Triodion*.* (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 1.	The table contains 35 columns of Sundays of the whole year, according to the number of 35 Paschal keys and 35 corresponding Paschal days. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 2.	At the top of the table, that is, in its 1st transverse row, all 35 Paschal keys are indicated, under each of which extends a row, or column, of Sundays of the whole year, with the indication of the day of the month within which these Sundays fall, and their dates or names. The names of the months are given outside the column, on the left side, and separated from one another by transverse step-like lines; the day of the month is given in the column, and the number or name of the Sunday - outside the column, on the right side of the table. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 3.	In the 7th row are given all 35 days on which the Sunday of the Publican may fall, on which the *Triodion* begins. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 4.	In the 8th row are given in double print all 35 Paschal days, corresponding to the 35 Paschal keys. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 5.	In the five rows that are between the Publican and the Paschal keys, are indicated all 35 Sundays that may fall between Theophany and the Publican, that is, the Sunday after Theophany and the four last ordinary ones (29th, 30th, 31st and 32nd). However, since not all these Sundays will always take place, the explanation of which we gave on p. 144 [→REF:p144] (for this depends on whether Pascha falls earlier or later), between these five Sundays some places may be unfilled, and this means that then the Sundays corresponding to them, indicated on the right side of the table, will not take place. For example, under the Paschal key "A" all places are unfilled; this means that none of these 5 Sundays will be taken, but immediately after Theophany - the *Triodion*. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 6.	Of the *Triodion* Sundays, we presented only three, that is, the middle one - the Resurrection, and the two extreme ones, that is [Page 250] the Publican and All Saints; the rest of the Sundays that are between them, we do not present, for one can conveniently keep them in memory even without the table, because usually they have proper names and services that correspond to these names. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 7.	For the Sundays preceding the Sunday of the Publican, we do not present either the tone or the Sunday Gospel, for they depend on the past Pascha, and therefore one must look for them at the end of the table, within the limits of January and February under the Paschal key of the past Pascha. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 8.	Next to some Sundays, Latin letters are printed in double type. This means that then, with the Gospel of this Sunday, another must also be combined, of one of the 10 specific Sundays of the *Menaion*, presented after the table with the corresponding Latin letters[^750]. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 9.	After this, if you want to find out which Sundays in a certain year should be taken between Theophany and the Publican, seek first of all the Paschal key of the following Pascha, using Table I or Table II, and under it in Table III you will find these Sundays; only, if the year is leap, follow immediately the next column. And if you prefer to find out which Sunday, tone and Sunday Gospel are to be taken on one of the Sundays after the Descent of the Holy Spirit, then having determined the Paschal key of this year and having gone down its column to the place and day of the Sunday, follow from it along the transverse row to the right side of the table and you will find all this. (under ### August (Серпень))
  H5: ##### 10.	Regarding the Apodosis of the Meeting, which we present in the last row of the table, know that under the key "A" the number "0" means that the Apodosis will not be then; under keys from "R" to the end, the number "9" means that the Apodosis will be on February 9, and under others - it will be on different days, depending on the keys; only in a leap year follow immediately the next column. Paschal keys must be determined from the following Pascha. (under ### August (Серпень))

  H5: ##### 1. The Sanctuary and the Holy Table (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 1. The sanctuary should be at least one step higher than the temple of the faithful. Within the sanctuary itself, however, there should be no steps leading to the holy table. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 2. The holy table is square, separated from the wall, situated in the middle of the sanctuary so that it can be walked around. Under the northern wall of the sanctuary is placed the table of proskomedia (oblation), and under the southern - another table, on which the sacred vestments are prepared. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
