  H5: ##### 3. The Artophorion, or tabernacle [kyvot], which is contained in the middle of the holy table (throne), must be constructed in the form of a small temple or tomb. It should not be too high and wide, so that by its dimensions it does not hinder the liturgical actions of the celebrant and concelebrants nor render them impossible. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 4. Candlesticks standing on the holy table should not obscure it, but it is proper to place one candlestick with one, two or three candles on both sides of the cross or tabernacle. It is also permitted to use a seven-branched candlestick with oil lamps, which is placed behind the holy table. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 5. It is not permitted to place on the holy table any reliquaries, nor flowers, natural or artificial, nor anything that is unnecessary for divine service. The book for divine service (*Liturgicon*) should be placed on a cushion, on the holy table, and not on a stand, unless it lies on an analogion on the left side of the throne. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 6. The iconostasis must have the icon of the Savior to the right of the holy doors[^754] and of the Theotokos - to the left, and other icons placed, according to custom, in their places. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 7. No one dares to have entry into the sanctuary except priests, deacons, clerics and servers. For the faithful who do not cooperate in the divine service, there is no place in the sanctuary, and even less so for women, regardless of origin and reason. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 8. The diskos should be sufficiently deep and provided with a rim; it may have a foot. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 9. The censer must have short chains so that it can be set in motion with a light swing. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 10. One should not use other liturgical vestments nor other liturgical cloths except those indicated in this Rubric for each divine service; the use of small bells, organs, large or small, i. e., harmoniums, is excluded. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 2. General Rules (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 11. A small bow, - during which, signing ourselves with the sign of the cross, we incline the head and shoulders slightly, however we do not extend the hands to the knees or even to the ground, before or after the sign of the cross, - we make: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 12. A great bow [prostration], during which, signing ourselves with the sign of the cross, we fall to the ground with our whole body, we make only during the Forty Days [Lent] and at the Liturgy of Presanctified Gifts. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 13. Prayers with extended and uplifted hands are recited only when this is explicitly prescribed, that is: at the prayer "No one is worthy" (at the Cherubic Hymn), at "Let us lift up our hearts" and at the Lord's Prayer. When extending the hands, the elbows should not rest on the sides, but the hands themselves should be freely lifted upwards. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 14. The hands, if nothing is prescribed about them, can be held in various ways: placed on the holy table or holding the *Liturgicon* with them, lowered down under the phelonion or folded on the breast, but not interlocking finger to finger. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 15. When the Deacon exclaims the litanies, he raises the orarion with three fingers of the right hand upwards, even if it is not always explicitly prescribed by the rubrics. Already at the beginning of the litanies, the Priest may begin to recite its prayer quietly. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 16. When the Deacon exclaims: "Bow your heads unto the Lord," all bow their head and shoulders. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 17. Servers in the sanctuary during the Divine Liturgy do not bend their knees, but the people, if they wish, may kneel. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 18. When the Priest blesses, he places the thumb of the right hand crosswise on the ring finger, keeps the index finger straight, and slightly bends the middle and little fingers. To receive the blessing, all bow their heads and shoulders and sign themselves with the sign of the cross. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 19. Regarding the opening and closing of doors and the drawing and undrawing of the curtain of the iconostasis, one must observe the following general rules: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 20. Not the Priest alone places incense into the censer, this may also be done by a server or the Deacon and then presented to the Priest for blessing. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 21. When the Priest at the divine service blesses the censer for the first time, he recites the prayer "Incense we offer to Thee," and subsequently - only the words: "Blessed is our God." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 22. When putting on and kissing vestments, it is necessary to follow the prescriptions given at the beginning of the Proskomedia (see nos. 103-106); at other divine services, having left aside the corresponding prayers, one must observe the same. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 23. If a Priest, having to celebrate a divine service, does not put on the sticharion, he must put on over his long garment also an outer one with wide sleeves, which is called in Greek rason, and in Slavonic - ryasa. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 24. No one is permitted to put on vestments proper to a rank higher than the one to which he belongs. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 25. The Deacon, while serving the Priest, stands to his right, unless explicitly prescribed otherwise. If there are two deacons, the first stands to the right, and the second - to the left of the Priest. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 26. Clerics (seminarians) who do not yet have diaconal ordination are not permitted to perform the diaconal service. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 27. What the Priest or Deacon exclaims, other concelebrants should not recite quietly, except for the exclamations that conclude prayers, unless prescribed otherwise. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 28. The full ceremonies are explained in that part which speaks about the celebration in concelebration with one deacon. In other parts, which speak about the service with two deacons, without a Deacon, with Priest-concelebrants, only that which is proper to these parts is explained fully. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 1. In Concelebration of One Deacon (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 29. The Deacon, holding the sticharion with the orarion, approaches the Priest and, bowing his head, says: "Bless, Master, the sticharion with the orarion." The Priest blesses, saying: "Blessed is our God." The (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 30. The Priest, having taken upon himself the epitrachelion over the ryasa, having previously blessed it, goes out through the northern doors and, standing before the closed holy doors, makes a small bow and exclaims: "Blessed is our God." After "Our Father," He exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 31. During the reading of the 103rd Psalm, the Priest before the holy doors recites quietly the Vespers (Lamp-lighting) Prayers[^756]. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 32. After the completion of the Kathisma, the Deacon, having gone out through the northern doors, sings the Small Litany and, after the exclamation of the Priest "For Thine is the dominion," makes a small bow and returns into the sanctuary[^757]. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 33. When the Choir begins to sing "Lord, I have cried," the Deacon takes the censer and, after the Priest blesses the incense, censes the holy table from the four sides, then the icon behind the altar, the icons of the sanctuary and the Priest[^758]. After this, having gone out of the sanctuary through the northern doors[^772], he comes before the holy doors, makes a bow, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the icon of the Savior; then, having made again a bow before the holy doors, censes the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, then - the choirs, right and left; He censes the people from the solea, after which He returns into the sanctuary through the southern doors. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 34. When the Choir begins to sing the last sticheron, the Priest puts on the phelonion, having blessed and kissed it, and, at "Glory," comes with the Deacon before the holy table. The holy doors are opened. The Deacon takes the censer and, after the Priest blesses the incense, together with him, who now lowers the phelonion, goes around the holy table, beginning from the right side and, preceded by candle-bearers, they go out through the northern doors. They stand before the holy doors: candle-bearers on opposite sides, one - near the icon of the Theotokos, the second - near the icon of the Savior, the Deacon - in the middle, and behind him - the Priest. The Priest and deacon bow their heads. The Deacon, holding the orarion with three fingers, says quietly: "Let us pray to the Lord," and the Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Entrance "In the evening and in the morning and at noonday." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 35. After the readings, the Priest returns to the throne, and the Deacon, having paid him respect, goes around the holy table, exits through the northern doors and, having stood before the closed holy doors, exclaims the litany "Let us all say," and the (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 36. The Choir begins to sing the Aposticha stichera, then - "Now lettest Thou," the Trisagion and, after "Our Father," the Priest exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 2. In Concelebration of Two Deacons (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 37. After the completion of the 103rd Psalm, the 1st deacon goes out through the northern doors, makes a small bow, pays respect to the Priest and exclaims the Great Litany, and after the Priest's exclamation "For to Thee belongs," makes a small bow and returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 38. After the Kathisma, the 2nd deacon goes out through the northern doors, exclaims the Small Litany, and after the exclamation "For Thine is the dominion," makes a small bow and returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary (see no. 32, note). (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 39. When the Choir begins to sing "Lord, I have cried," the deacons take censers and, having put in incense, approach the Priest. The Priest blesses the incense and both deacons cense: the throne - first from the front, then the 1st deacon - from the right side, and the 2nd - from the left, and again together - from the east, then - the icon behind the altar, other icons of the sanctuary and the Priest. After this, they go out of the sanctuary, the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern, come before the holy doors and, having made a bow, cense the icons of the iconostasis, the 1st deacon begins from the icon of the Savior, and the 2nd - from the icon of the Theotokos, then the 1st deacon - the right choir, and the 2nd - the left. They cense the people from the solea, the 1st - those on the right, and the 2nd - those on the left side. Then they return into the sanctuary, the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 40. When the Choir begins the last sticheron, the Priest with the deacons come at "Glory" (see no. 34) before the holy table. The holy doors are opened. The deacons take censers, the Priest blesses the incense. Then they go out through the northern doors: first - the 2nd deacon, after him - the 1st, and lastly - the Priest. They stand before the holy doors: the 2nd deacon - to the left of the Priest, and the 1st - to the right. The (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 41. After the readings, the Priest returns before the holy table, the deacons bow to the Priest and go out through their doors, that is the 2nd deacon - through the northern, and the 1st - through the southern, stand before the holy doors and the 1st exclaims the litany "Let us all say," and the 2nd - "Let us complete," after which the (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 42. After the Troparia, while the holy doors are open, the deacons go out, each - through his doors, and stand before the holy doors, one - to the right, and the other - to the left. The 1st deacon, standing near the icon of the Savior, looks towards the people, raises the orarion and exclaims: "Wisdom." When the Priest says the dismissal, both deacons raise their oraria and hold them raised until the conclusion of the dismissal, then return through their doors into the sanctuary and, having closed the holy doors, make a small bow before the throne and take off their holy vestments. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 3. Without a Deacon (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 43. A Priest celebrating Vespers without a Deacon does and recites everything that belongs to the Deacon, with the exception of the calls "Let us pray to the Lord," "Bless," observing the following: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 44. He sings the Great Litany before the holy doors and after the exclamation returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 45. After the Kathisma, He sings the Small Litany. At "Lord, I have cried" He censes, as was noted (comp. no. 33). After the censing, He enters through the southern doors into the sanctuary and gives away the censer. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 46. For the Entrance, he takes the censer or the Holy Gospel, goes around the holy table from the right side, exits through the northern doors, bows his head before the holy doors and recites the Prayer of the Entrance "In the evening and in the morning"; with his right hand he blesses the entrance, kisses the Gospel, if he has it, and then exclaims: "Wisdom, arise," making the sign of the cross with the censer or lifting the Holy Gospel with both hands, and returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary. During the singing of "O Gladsome Light," he censes, as was noted (see no. 33), returns into the sanctuary and stands before the throne. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 47. After the hymn "O Gladsome Light," he approaches the holy doors and, looking at the people, says: "Let us be attentive" and blesses the people: "Peace be to all" and adds: "Wisdom, let us be attentive." Then, if there be readings, he turns to the throne, goes from the right side behind the throne and exclaims before the title of each paremia: "Wisdom," and after the title: "Let us be attentive." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 48. After the readings, he returns to the holy table and exclaims before it the litanies "Let us all say" and "Let us complete." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 49. After the Troparia, he exclaims himself, having turned to the people: "Wisdom" and, standing in the holy doors, says the dismissal, as was noted (see no. 36, note). (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 4. In Concelebration of Priests (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 50. Concelebrating priests, whose number should be even, put on before the Entrance only epitrachelia and phelons over their ryasas and stand on the sides of the holy table, and not before it, where only the principal celebrant and deacons stand; they stand according to the order of their dignity or ordination: the first priest - on the right side of the throne, the second - on the left, the third - again on the right, and so on. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 51. For the Entrance, all go out, having made a small bow, through the northern doors; they lower their phelons and hands down and stand, in front - the younger ones, in a double row before the holy doors, facing one another, and the principal celebrant stands in the middle, behind all, and recites the Prayer of the Entrance himself (see no. 34). (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 52. The exclamation "For a merciful" is said by the principal celebrant, and "For Thou Art a good God" and others may be exclaimed by the 1st concelebrant, but "Blessed and glorified be" - the principal celebrant takes. After the dismissal, all make a small bow before the holy table, depart and take off the sacred vestments. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 1. In Concelebration of One Deacon (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 53. The Deacon, holding the sticharion and orarion, approaches the Priest, bows his head and says: "Bless, Master." The Priest blesses, saying: "Blessed is our God." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 54. The Priest puts on the ryasa the epitrachelion, having previously blessed it, and puts on the phelonion, having blessed and kissed [it], and opens the holy doors. The Deacon goes out through the northern doors, stands with a lighted candle before the holy doors and says: "Bless, Master," after which He returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 55. Elsewhere, however, there exists a custom that the Deacon, after "Bless, Master," returns into the sanctuary and, having taken the censer and received the blessing from the Priest, censes everything himself, and the Priest stands at this time before the throne. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 56. The Priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion, goes out in the epitrachelion alone through the northern doors before the holy doors and there reads the Vespers Prayers. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 57. After the exclamation "Blessed and glorified be the dominion of Thy kingdom," the Priest with the cross in his hand, and the Deacon - with the censer go out through the northern doors or, if there exists a custom and the holy doors be open, the Priest goes out[^762] through the holy, and the Deacon - through the northern doors. From here they go into the narthex or, if there is none, to a place near the entrance doors of the temple, preceded by the Choir and candle-bearers. The Deacon, having received the blessing of the incense, censes the holy icons, if they be in the narthex, then - the Priest, the Choir and the people. After the completion of the Litiya stichera, the Deacon exclaims the litany "O God, save Thy people." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 58. After the call of the (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 59. After the completion of the prayer, the Choir sings: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and the Priest enters with the Deacon through the southern doors into the sanctuary, or the Priest - through the holy (see no. 57), and the Deacon - through the southern, and they stand before the holy table. After this, the Choir reads the 33rd Psalm "I will bless the Lord" until "shall not be deprived of any good thing." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 60. If Matins is added directly to Vespers with Vigil, the Priest after the exclamation "The blessing of the Lord be upon you" closes the holy doors and takes off the phelonion. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 2. In Concelebration of Two Deacons (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 61. The Priest puts on the epitrachelion and puts on the phelonion, and the deacons - sticharia and oraria. The Priest opens the holy doors, and the 1st deacon with a lighted candle goes out through the southern doors, stands before the holy [doors] and, having made a small bow, calls: "Command," and through the southern doors returns again into the sanctuary (see no. 54). (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 62. The Priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion and in the epitrachelion alone goes out through the northern doors before the holy doors, where he reads the Vespers Prayers (see no. 31, note). (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 63. After the exclamation "Blessed and glorified be," the Priest goes out with the cross in his hand, and the deacons - with censers through the northern doors or, if according to custom the holy doors are open, the Priest - through the holy (see no. 57), and the deacons - through their doors. From here they proceed to the narthex or, if there is none, to a place near the entrance to the temple, preceded by the Choir and candle-bearers. The deacons, after the blessing of the incense by the Priest, cense, both from their side, the holy icons, if they be in the narthex, the Priest, the Choir and the present people. After the completion of the Litiya stichera, the deacons sing alternately the prayers: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 64. During the singing of the Aposticha Troparion, the deacons cense the tetrapod from the four sides: first both - from the front, then the 1st deacon - from the right side, the 2nd deacon - from the left, then both go behind the tetrapod and cense it from the east, and after this - only the Rector and the Priest. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 65. Before the blessing of the loaves the (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 66. After the completion of the prayer the Choir sings: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and the Priest with the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd deacon - through the northern doors enter the sanctuary and stand before the holy table. The Choir reads the 33rd Psalm "I will bless the Lord" until "shall not be deprived of any good thing." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 3. Without a Deacon (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 67. The Priest in epitrachelion and phelonion opens the holy doors, censes the holy table from four sides and everything else, as noted (see no. 54). Turning to the holy doors he says in a half-voice: "Lord, bless." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 68. During the reading of the 103rd Psalm, the Priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion and in the epitrachelion alone goes out through the northern doors, reads before the holy doors the Vespers (Lamp-lighting) Prayers, there also he sings the Great Litany. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 69. After the exclamation "Blessed and glorified be," the Priest goes out with the cross in his hands through the northern or, if there exists a custom, holy doors. From here he goes to the very narthex or to a place near the entrance doors. The Choir and candle-bearers also gather in the narthex. The Priest takes the censer, censes the icons, if they be in the narthex, the Choir and the people. After the completion of the Litiya stichera he himself reads the litanies. After the completion of this, he returns to the temple, approaches the tetrapod, censes it from four sides and after this - only the Rector. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 4. In Concelebration of Priests (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 70. The concelebrants make the Entrance and after it return to the throne, as noted before in the rubric of Vespers without Vigil (see no. 51). Having made a small bow, they do not depart, but wait for the Litiya. The exclamation "For a merciful" is exclaimed by the principal celebrant, another "For Thou Art a good God" may be exclaimed by the 1st concelebrant, but the exclamation "Blessed and glorified be the dominion of Thy kingdom" - the principal celebrant. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 71. Then, during the procession at the Litiya, all make a small bow before the holy table and go out, in front - the younger ones, whether through the northern, or, if there exists a custom, through the holy doors, having lowered their phelons and hands down. In the narthex or at the place near the church doors they stand in two rows, one beside the other, and the principal celebrant - in the middle, behind all. The Deacon or deacons stand near the principal celebrant. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 72. At the blessing of loaves, the concelebrants are arranged thus: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 73. If after Vespers follows directly Matins, then they, having taken off the sacred vestments, do not go out of the sanctuary, but remain on the seats under the apse until the Polyeleos itself. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 1. In Concelebration of One Deacon (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 74. If before this there was no Vigil, then Matins begins thus[^764]: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 75. The Priest puts on the ryasa the epitrachelion, having previously blessed it, goes before the holy doors and begins: "Blessed is our God." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 76. During the reading of the Six Psalms[^766] the Priest, standing before the holy table, begins to recite the Matins Prayers. After the first three psalms, he goes out through the northern doors and, having stood before the holy doors, continues to read the Matins Prayers. After the completion of the psalms, the Priest responds to the bow of the Deacon and, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, stands before the holy table. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 77. After the Troparia, the Reader reads the regular Kathisma, after which the Deacon [exclaims] before the holy doors - the Small Litany, and the Priest exclaims: "For Thine is the dominion." Then the Reader reads the second regular Kathisma, the Deacon - the second Small Litany, and the Priest - the exclamation "For Thou Art a good God." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 78. After the Gradual [Gradual], the holy doors are opened, and the Deacon approaches the holy doors and, looking at the people, exclaims: "Let us be attentive." The Priest, dressed in the phelonion, blesses the people: "Peace be to all." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 79. After the reading, the Priest kisses the closed book and carries it to the middle of the temple, where he places it on the tetrapod, and he himself returns through the holy doors into the sanctuary. The Deacon reads "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," if it is Sunday, or the Reader - the 50th Psalm "Have mercy on me, O God." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 80. If there was a Vigil, the Priest after the exclamation "By the grace," holding the cross in his hands, goes out through the holy [doors], and the Deacon with the oil, blessed at Vespers, and the anointing brush, - through the northern doors and they stand on the right side of the tetrapod. There the Priest anoints crosswise the forehead of everyone who approaches to kiss the Holy Gospel. After the completion of the anointing [Myrovanne] they return to the sanctuary: the Priest - through the holy, and the Deacon - through the southern doors and, having closed the holy doors, the Priest takes off the phelonion. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 81. After the third and sixth Odes, the Deacon sings the Small Litany either before the holy doors, or near the tetrapod, if the anointing was not yet finished, and the Priest exclaims the exclamation either from the sanctuary, or from the tetrapod. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 82. After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," the Deacon opens the holy doors. The Priest in the phelonion stands before the throne, the Deacon stands to his right and, after the completion of the last sticheron, the Priest, turned to the east, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the Light." The Great Doxology is sung. Then, also according to custom, the Troparion is recited, and the Priest, preceded by the Deacon, carries the Holy Gospel from the tetrapod into the sanctuary and places it on the holy table. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 83. After this, the Deacon stands at his usual place and exclaims the litanies "Have mercy on us, O God" and "Let us complete." The Priest blesses the people and exclaims: "Peace be to all." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 84. After the dismissal, the Reader immediately begins the 1st Hour: "Come, let us worship" (3) and the psalms. After "Our Father," the Priest in the epitrachelion: "For Thine is the kingdom." Reader - Kontakion and "Lord, have mercy" (40). The Priest goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors and before the holy [doors], turned to the east, loudly: "Thou Who at all times". (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 2. In Concelebration of Two Deacons (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 85. At the end of the Six Psalms, the 1st deacon goes out through the northern doors and exclaims the Great Litany, as well as the verses at "God is the Lord" and, having made a small bow, returns into the sanctuary through the southern doors. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 86. For the anointing of the faithful, the 1st deacon goes out with the Priest, and the 2nd exclaims the Small Litany after the third Ode, and also - after the sixth Ode. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 87. After the ninth Ode, the 1st Deacon exclaims the Small Litany. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 88. After the Praises stichera, the holy doors are opened. The 1st deacon stands before the holy table to the right of the Priest, and the 2nd - to the left. After the Great Doxology, all, having made a small bow, go to the tetrapod, the Priest takes the Holy Gospel and, preceded by the deacons, returns into the sanctuary. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 88. After this, the deacons go out: the 1st Deacon exclaims the first litany "Have mercy on us, O God," and the 2nd - the second "Let us complete." (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 3. Without a Deacon (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 89. A Priest who serves Matins without a Deacon does and says everything that belongs to the Deacon, considering this: (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 90. After the completion of the Six Psalms, He does not return into the sanctuary, but, standing before the holy doors, exclaims the Great Litany and its exclamation: "For to Thee belongs," as well as the verses during the singing by the Choir of "God is the Lord." After the completion of this, He makes a small bow and returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 91. After the Katavasia of the 8th Ode, he goes out in the epitrachelion through the northern doors, stands before the icon of the Theotokos and calls: "The Theotokos and Mother of Light," then censes, as noted above (see no. 33). After the censing, he returns through the southern doors into the sanctuary, exclaims the Small Litany and its exclamation. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 92. After the Praises stichera, at "Glory," He puts on the phelonion, opens the holy doors and, after the last sticheron, exclaims: "Glory to Thee, Who Hast shown us the Light." Everything else takes place as was presented (see nos. 82-83). (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 93. After the dismissal, the Priest closes the holy doors, takes off the phelonion and the 1st Hour begins. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 4. In Concelebration of Priests (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 94. Concelebrants, in an even number, put on epitrachelia over their ryasas and put on phelons during the reading of the Kathisma and stand on the sides of the holy table, as at Vespers (see no. 50). During the singing of the Polyeleos, all go out, having made a small bow, through the holy doors, having lowered the phelonion and hands down, in front - the younger ones. Two first ones carry the icon of the feast or saint, if it has not yet been placed on the left side of the tetrapod between both choirs, and place it on the tetrapod; all stand on the sides of the tetrapod in two rows, facing one another, the principal celebrant stands in the middle before the tetrapod, and to his right - the Deacon. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 95. Then all sing the Magnification. When the Choir repeats the Magnification, the Priest, preceded by the Deacon with a lighted candle, censes from four sides the icon of the feast or saint on the tetrapod, the holy table, the sanctuary, the iconostasis, the concelebrants, both choirs, the people and once more the icon of the feast, but only from the front. If, however, the usual censing takes place before the Gospel, then at the Magnification the Priest censes only the icon itself. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 96. After the Magnifications all, the younger ones - in front, return through the holy doors into the sanctuary before the holy table. After the Gospel all, having made a small bow, depart and take off their vestments. (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 1. Beginning (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 2. Sung Kathismata (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 3. Bringing out the Gospel (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 4. Magnification of the Theotokos (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 5. Laudatory Prayers (under ## I. Introductory Remarks)
  H5: ##### 1. In Concelebration of One Deacon (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 97. According to the ancient custom of the Eastern Church, only one Holy Liturgy may be celebrated at the same holy table. However, one may depart from this rule if the Divine Liturgy must be repeated for the benefit of the faithful. If there is one or several secondary churches in the parish, then the more solemn Liturgy is celebrated in the main sanctuary, and other liturgies should be celebrated rather in the side sanctuaries. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 98. The Eucharistic bread does not absolutely have to be baked on the day the Holy Sacrifice is offered. The same bread can be kept for several days and used at several liturgies, provided that all the ceremonies of the Proskomedia are observed over it or over a part of it, cutting the bread and removing the Lamb and particles according to the prescription. Also, it is not necessary to always use five prosphora, if the Lamb and all particles can be taken from one and the same prosphora. The custom of using five prosphora may be kept, if one pleases, when celebrating more solemn liturgies (see no. 179). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 99. The Priest who wishes to serve the Sacrament must first of all be reconciled with all and hold nothing against anyone, keep his heart as far as possible from evil thoughts, be continent already from the evening and keep the fast until the very time of the service and prepare himself for the offering of the sacrifice by prayers (see no. 190). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 100. At the prescribed time, the Priest enters the church and, together with the Deacon, bows three times before the holy doors to the east. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 101. After this the Priest and deacon return before the holy doors, and the Priest, bowing his head, recites the prayer "O Lord, stretch forth Thy hand." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 102. Having entered the sanctuary, they make three small bows before the holy table and kiss: the Priest - the Holy Gospel, and the Deacon - the holy table and, if there exists a custom, both - the altar cross. Having arrived at the place where the sacred vestments are kept, each takes his sticharion in his hands and makes three small bows to the east, saying to himself: "O God, be merciful to me a sinner." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 103. Then the Deacon approaches the Priest and, holding in his right hand the sticharion with the orarion and cuffs and bowing his head, says: "Bless, Master, the sticharion with the orarion." And the Priest blesses, saying: "Blessed is our God." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 104. Then the Deacon withdraws and, at some place in the sanctuary, puts on the sticharion, praying thus: "My soul shall rejoice in the Lord." Having kissed the orarion, He places it on His left shoulder. Putting the cuff on the right hand, He says: "Thy right hand, O Lord," and on the left: "Thy hands have made me." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 105. The Priest, in the right part of the sanctuary, vests thus: having taken the sticharion in his left hand, he makes three small bows to the east, as was said about this, and, blessing it, says: "Blessed is our God." Then He puts it on, saying: "My soul shall rejoice in the Lord." Having taken the epitrachelion and blessed it, He puts it on Himself and says: "Blessed is God, Who pours out His grace." Having taken the zone [belt], He girds Himself, saying: "Blessed is God, Who girds me with strength." Putting the cuff on the right hand, He says: "Thy right hand, O Lord," and on the left: "Thy hands have made me." Then He takes the epigonation [nabedrenyk], if He has one, and, having blessed and kissed it, hangs it over His right knee, saying: "Gird Thy sword." After this, having taken and blessed the phelonion, He kisses it and says: "Thy priests, O Lord." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 106. One may vest also in the sacristy. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 107. Dressed thus in vestments, the Priest and deacon go to the table of oblation, where they wash their hands, saying: "I will wash my hands among the innocent." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 108. Then they make three small bows before the table of oblation and say: "O God, be merciful to me a sinner," "Thou Hast redeemed us." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 109. The Deacon, having taken wine and water, says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 110. Then the Priest takes the second prosphora in his hands, however if there is no second or other prosphora, he finishes the prescribed liturgical actions on one, saying: "In honor and memory" and, having taken a particle, places it to the right of the holy bread, near its middle, saying: "At Thy right hand stood the Queen." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 111. Then, having taken the third prosphora, He says: "Of the honorable, heavenly," takes the first particle, places it to the left of the holy bread, beginning the first row. Then He says: "Of the honorable, glorious Prophet, Forerunner." The taken particle He places under the first and says: "Of the holy, glorious," and then places the third particle under the second, finishing the first row. Then He says: "Of our holy Fathers"; having taken the fourth particle, He places it next to the first, beginning the second row. Then He says: "Of the holy Apostle, Protomartyr" and, having taken the fifth particle, places it under the first [of the second row], with which He begins the second row. After this He says: "Of our venerable and God-bearing [Fathers]" and, having taken the sixth particle, places it under the second [of the second row], finishing the second row. Then He says: "Of the holy and wonderworking Unmercenaries." Having taken the seventh particle, He places it next to the first [of the second row], beginning the third row. Then again He says: "Of the holy and righteous Ancestors of God" and places the eighth particle under the first [of the third row]. He takes the ninth particle, saying: "Of Saint (Name) and all saints," and places it at the end of the third row, finishing it. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 112. After this He takes the fourth prosphora and says: "Remember, O Master, Lover of mankind, our most holy [Pontiff]," and places the taken particle below the holy bread. Then He mentions by name the living, Whom He wishes, and for each name takes out a particle, saying: "Remember, O Lord, (Name)." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 113. Finally He takes the fifth prosphora, mentions the Bishop who ordained him, and by name the deceased, whom he wishes. For each name he takes out a particle, saying: "Remember, O Lord, the soul." Then He says: "And of all in the hope of resurrection" and takes out a particle. After this He says: "Remember, O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy compassions" and takes out a particle. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 114. He gathers the particles on the diskos under the holy bread, so that they be together and so that none falls off[^768]. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 115. The Deacon takes the censer and, having put in incense, says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 116. Then the Deacon says: "For the precious gifts offered," and the Priest, making no sign of blessing, recites the Prayer of the Offering: "O God, our God," after which He makes the dismissal there, speaking thus: "Glory to Thee, O Christ God." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 117. After the dismissal of the Proskomedia, the Priest goes before the holy table, and the Deacon censes the table of oblation three times, then the holy table from the four sides, saying quietly: "In the tomb" and the 50th(51st) Psalm, after which he censes the icon behind the altar. He goes out of the sanctuary through the northern doors, comes before the holy doors and, having bowed before them, censes the icons of the southern part of the iconostasis, beginning from the icon of the Savior, then - the icons of the northern part, beginning from the icon of the Theotokos, and then - the choirs, right and left. He censes the faithful either from the solea, or, if there is a custom, going through the church to the very narthex. Having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, he censes the holy table once more, only from the front, and likewise - the celebrant (see nos. 9, 20, 33, 39, 54; also no. 177). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 118. Then He gives the censer to the server, and himself stands to the right of the Priest. Standing together before the holy table, they recite "O Heavenly King." Then they bow three times, saying: "Glory to God in the highest" (2), "O Lord, open my lips," then they kiss: the Priest - the Holy Gospel, and the Deacon - the holy table (see no. 102). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 119. And the Priest, standing in the middle before the holy table and making the sign of the cross over it with the Holy Gospel[^769], begins loudly: "Blessed is the kingdom." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 120. After the completion of the Antiphon, the Deacon returns to his place and, having made a small bow, exclaims: "Again and again." And the Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Second Antiphon: "O Lord our God, save Thy people." Exclamation: "For Thine is the dominion." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 121. Then the Deacon, having returned again to the usual place, sings: "Again and again," and the Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Third Antiphon "Thou Who Hast given us." Exclamation: "For Thou Art a good." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 122. The Third Antiphon or the Beatitudes, if it is Sunday, is sung. When the singers reach "Glory," the Priest and deacon, standing before the holy table, make three small bows. Then the Priest takes the Holy Gospel, gives it to the Deacon and lowers the phelonion. Both go around the holy table from the right side and, having gone out through the northern doors, preceded by candle-bearers, make the Little Entrance. Having come before the holy doors, the candle-bearers stand on opposite sides: one - near the icon of the Theotokos, and the other - of the Savior, the Priest - in the middle, and the Deacon - slightly in front of him, to the right. Both bow their heads and the Deacon says in a low voice: "Let us pray to the Lord," and the Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Entrance "O Master, Lord our God." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 123. The Priest recites quietly the Prayer of the Trisagion "O Holy God." When the singers reach the last troparion, the Deacon bows his head before the Priest and, holding the orarion with three fingers in his hand, says: "Bless, Master, the time of the Thrice-Holy," and the Priest blesses him and exclaims: "For Holy Art Thou, our God." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 124. Then the Deacon says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 125. After the completion of the Trisagion hymn, the Deacon approaches the holy doors and, holding the orarion with three fingers of the raised right hand, says: "Let us be attentive," and the Priest blesses the people and exclaims: "Peace be to all." Then again the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 126. During the singing of "Alleluia," the Deacon, having taken the censer and put incense into it, approaches the Priest and says: "Bless, Master, the incense." Having received the blessing from the Priest, who says: "Blessed is our God," He censes around the holy table, the whole sanctuary, the Priest, the icons of the sanctuary, the icons of the iconostasis, both choirs and the people (see nos. 33 and 75). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 127. After the reading of the Gospel, the Priest says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 128. The Priest recites quietly the First Prayer of the Faithful "We thank Thee." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 129. During the singing of the Cherubic Hymn, the Deacon, having taken the censer and put incense into it, approaches the Priest, saying: "Bless, Master, the incense" and, having received the blessing: "Blessed is God," recites the 50th(51st) Psalm and censes around the holy table, the icon behind the altar, the icons of the iconostasis, both choirs and the people. At the end, having returned through the southern doors into the sanctuary, He censes the Priest[^773]. The Priest, having lifted his hands up, recites quietly the prayer "No one is worthy." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 130. The Priest, having entered through the holy doors, places the holy chalice on the throne; he also takes from the Deacon[^776] the holy diskos and places it on the throne, to the left of the chalice. Having removed the small veils from the diskos and chalice, he places them on the throne, and with the large veil, that is the aer, which he takes from the Deacon's shoulder and censes over the censer, he covers the Holy Gifts, saying: "The noble Joseph." Then, having taken the censer from the Deacon's hands, he censes the Holy Gifts crosswise, saying: "Do good, O Lord" (see nos. 9, 20, 149). During this prayer the holy doors are closed, and the candle-bearers, having made together a bow before the holy doors, withdraw to their places. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 131. Having given away the censer, the Priest lowers the phelonion and, bowing his head, says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 132. The Deacon, having bowed to the Priest, goes out through the northern doors and, standing at the usual place, says: "Let us complete." The Priest reads quietly the Prayer of the Offering, after the placing of the divine gifts on the holy table "O Lord God Almighty." Exclamation: "Through the compassions." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 133. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 134. After this the Deacon stands to the right of the Priest and, if necessary, takes into his hands the fan or small veil and silently, with all attention and reverence, fans over the Holy Gifts. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 135. The Priest recites quietly the prayer "Remembering this," and the Deacon, crossing his hands, taking with the right - the holy diskos, and with the left lifting slightly the holy chalice, makes the sign of the cross over the antimension [iliton]. The Priest exclaims: "Thine own of Thine own." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 136. The Priest recites quietly the prayer "That to those who shall partake" and, taking the censer, exclaims: "Especially for our most holy," thrice censes the Gifts from the front of the table. Then he covers the holy chalice with the small veil or purificator. The Choir sings "It is truly meet" or, instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos of the feast that falls. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 137. The Deacon, having received the blessing from the Priest, makes a small bow, goes around the holy table and goes out through the northern doors and, standing at his usual place, exclaims: "Having commemorated all the saints." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 138. The Deacon makes a small bow and enters through the southern doors into the sanctuary and, having stood to the right of the Priest, makes once more a small bow and says: "Break, Master, the Holy Bread," and the Priest breaks it attentively and piously into four parts in this manner: first he divides it into two parts and the part which he holds in the right hand, that is the one that has the inscriptions "XC" and "KA", he places on the diskos, then breaks across between the letters "IC" and "NI" the part which he holds in the left hand and places the particle "IC" at the top of the diskos, and the particle "NI" - on the left; having taken then the second half, he breaks it in the middle between the letters "XC" and "KA" and places the particle "XC" at the bottom of the diskos, and the particle "KA" on the right edge of the diskos, so that the particles form the sign of the cross on the diskos. While doing this he says: "Broken and distributed." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 139. If there is the rite of the zeon [warm water], the Deacon, presenting the warm water, says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 140. The Deacon goes around the holy table and, if necessary, washes the palm of his right hand and, at the words of the Priest "Deacon, approach," approaches him from the left side, bows to him, asking forgiveness. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 141. Afterwards the Priest, bowing down, takes the chalice in his right hand, and in his left a small purificator, holding it under his chin, and communicates of the Blood from the chalice, saying: "Of the precious and holy Blood of the Lord and God." And wipes His lips, as well as the edge of the holy chalice, with the small purificator, saying: "Lo, this has touched my lips." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 142. The Deacon makes a small bow, takes with piety the chalice from the Priest and approaches the holy doors and, lifting the holy chalice, shows it to the people, saying: "With fear of God and with faith draw near." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 143. The Priest places the holy chalice on the throne and censes it three times, praying quietly: "Be Thou exalted, O God, above the heavens." Then He gives the censer to the Deacon and, having taken the holy diskos, places it on the Deacon's head, and the Deacon, holding it with piety in front and looking towards the holy doors, saying nothing, goes straight between the holy table and the holy doors to the table of oblation and places the holy diskos on it. After this he waits for the Priest at the same place. And the Priest, having made a small bow and taken the holy chalice, turned to the holy doors, looks at the people and says quietly: "Blessed is our God," and then exclaims: "Always, now and ever." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 144. The Deacon, having untied the orarion, as custom prescribes, goes out through the northern doors and, standing at the usual place, exclaims: "Arise! Having partaken of the divine." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 145. After the completion of the psalm and the distribution of the antidoron, the Priest stands in the holy doors and, blessing the people, exclaims: "The blessing of the Lord be upon you." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 146. In seminaries, houses of study and other places where there are many priests, the Priest who serves as deacon performs his diaconal service fully, namely: he receives Holy Communion at that Liturgy at which he serves as deacon, from the hands of the Priest, and consumes what remains after communion. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 2. In Concelebration of Two Deacons (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 147. On solemn feasts two deacons serve the Priest or concelebrants. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 148. At the appropriate time the Priest enters the church and, together with both deacons, makes three small bows to the east before the holy doors. Then they do everything as at the Liturgy in concelebration with one deacon, and both respond to the Priest. After the prayer "O Lord, stretch forth Thy hand" the Priest, and with him the deacons, bow to the right and left choirs and depart into the sanctuary: the Priest and the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd deacon - through the northern doors, saying "I will enter into Thy house." Having arrived in the sanctuary, they make three small bows before the holy table and kiss: the Priest - the Holy Gospel, and the deacons - the holy table and all, if there exists a custom, - the altar cross, which is on the holy table. Having vested, they wash their hands and prepare everything for the Proskomedia. Only the 1st Deacon says: "Bless, Master" and everything else, and the Proskomedia is celebrated, according to custom. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 149. After the dismissal of the Proskomedia, the deacons cense the offering with three swings, and then go together with the Priest before the holy table, saying quietly: "In the tomb with the body" and the rest, they cense together it from the front, then the 1st deacon - the right side of the holy table, and the 2nd deacon - the left, and again both together - from the east, then - the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary, as well as the 1st deacon - the cross, and the 2nd deacon - the icon of the Theotokos, if they be behind the throne (see no. 33). Then they go out of the sanctuary: the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors. Having come before the holy doors, they cense the icons of the iconostasis: the 1st deacon begins from the icon of the Savior, and the 2nd - from the icon of the Theotokos, after this the 1st deacon - the right, and the 2nd - the left choirs. They cense the faithful, one from the right, and the other from the left side whether from the solea, or, if there exists a custom, - in the church itself, going to the very narthex, after which they enter the sanctuary: the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors and cense once more the throne from the front with three swings and likewise the Priest. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 150. Having given away the censers to the server, both approach the Priest and do everything as at the Liturgy in concelebration with one deacon. They go out: the 1st deacon - through the southern, and the 2nd - through the northern doors and, having stood at their places before the holy doors, make three small bows, saying quietly thrice: "O Lord, open my lips." After this the 1st deacon begins: "Bless, Master." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 151. The first deacon sings the Great Litany. During the singing by the Choir of the first Antiphon or Typika [Psalm], he makes a small bow, departs from his place and stands before the icon of Christ, holding the orarion with three fingers of His right hand, according to custom (see no. 120). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 152. When the singers, who are singing the third Antiphon or the Beatitudes, reach "Glory" or the last verses of the Beatitudes, the Priest and deacons make three small bows. Then the Priest hands the Holy Gospel to the 1st deacon, and himself lowers the phelonion (see no. 34). The Priest with the 1st deacon go around the holy table from the right side, during which the 2nd Deacon takes the censer, and all go out through the northern doors, preceded by candle-bearers: first goes, censing, the 2nd deacon, then - the 1st deacon, carrying the Holy Gospel, and behind them - the Priest. Having approached the holy doors, the candle-bearers stand on opposite sides: one - near the icon of the Theotokos, and the other - near the icon of the Savior, the 1st deacon - on the right, and the 2nd - on the left side, and the Priest stands in the middle behind them. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 153. When the singers reach the last troparion, the 1st Deacon says: "Bless, Master, the time of the Thrice-Holy," and the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 154. After the completion of the Trisagion hymn, the deacons approach the holy doors and the 1st Deacon says: "Let us be attentive." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 155. During the singing of "Alleluia," the deacons, having taken censers and put in incense, approach the Priest and say: "Bless, Master, the incense" and, having received the blessing, cense the holy table first from the front, then the 1st deacon - from the right side, and the 2nd - from the left and again both together - from the east, then - the icon behind the altar and other icons of the sanctuary, the 2nd deacon also - the oblation, and both - everything else, according to custom. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 156. Then the deacons go out through their doors and stand at the designated places, and the 1st deacon begins: "Let us all say." Then the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 157. During the singing of the Cherubic Hymn, the deacons, having taken censers, put in incense and received the blessing, recite the 50th(51st) Psalm and cense the throne, the sanctuary and everything else, as was noted above concerning this (see no. 149). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 158. The (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 159. During the exclamation of the words of consecration, the deacons together with the Priest point with the orarion to the holy diskos, and then - to the holy chalice. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 160. During the singing of "It is truly meet," the deacons cense the throne from the sides, each - from his own, and from the east and commemorate the deceased, whom they wish. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 161. The Deacon makes a small bow and enters through the southern doors into the sanctuary and, having stood to the right of the Priest, makes once more a small bow and says: "Break, Master, the Holy Bread," and the Priest breaks it attentively and piously into four parts in this manner: first he divides it into two parts and the part which he holds in the right hand, that is the one that has the inscriptions "XC" and "KA", he places on the diskos, then breaks across between the letters "IC" and "NI" the part which he holds in the left hand and places the particle "IC" at the top of the diskos, and the particle "NI" - on the left; having taken then the second half, he breaks it in the middle between the letters "XC" and "KA" and places the particle "XC" at the bottom of the diskos, and the particle "KA" on the right edge of the diskos, so that the particles form the sign of the cross on the diskos. While doing this he says: "Broken and distributed." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 162. If there is the rite of the zeon [warm water], the Deacon, presenting the warm water, says to the (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 163. The Deacon goes around the holy table and, if necessary, washes the palm of his right hand and, at the words of the Priest "Deacon, approach," approaches him from the left side, bows to him, asking forgiveness. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 164. Afterwards the Priest, bowing down, takes the chalice in his right hand, and in his left a small purificator, holding it under his chin, and communicates of the Blood from the chalice, saying: "Of the precious and holy Blood of the Lord and God." And wipes His lips, as well as the edge of the holy chalice, with the small purificator, saying: "Lo, this has touched my lips." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 165. The Deacon makes a small bow, takes with piety the chalice from the Priest and approaches the holy doors and, lifting the holy chalice, shows it to the people, saying: "With fear of God and with faith draw near." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 166. The Priest places the holy chalice on the throne and censes it three times, praying quietly: "Be Thou exalted, O God, above the heavens." Then He gives the censer to the Deacon and, having taken the holy diskos, places it on the Deacon's head, and the Deacon, holding it with piety in front and looking towards the holy doors, saying nothing, goes straight between the holy table and the holy doors to the table of oblation and places the holy diskos on it. After this he waits for the Priest at the same place. And the Priest, having made a small bow and taken the holy chalice, turned to the holy doors, looks at the people and says quietly: "Blessed is our God," and then exclaims: "Always, now and ever." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 167. The Deacon, having untied the orarion, as custom prescribes, goes out through the northern doors and, standing at the usual place, exclaims: "Arise! Having partaken of the divine." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 168. After the completion of the psalm and the distribution of the antidoron, the Priest stands in the holy doors and, blessing the people, exclaims: "The blessing of the Lord be upon you." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 169. In seminaries, houses of study and other places where there are many priests, the Priest who serves as deacon performs his diaconal service fully, namely: he receives Holy Communion at that Liturgy at which he serves as deacon, from the hands of the Priest, and consumes what remains after communion. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 3. Without a Deacon (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 170. A Priest who serves without a Deacon performs and recites all that which belongs to the Deacon, considering the following: (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 171. He does not say "Bless, Master," "Let us pray to the Lord," "Take up, Master," etc., with the exception of what is said before the Prayer behind the Ambo, that is "Let us pray to the Lord." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 172. After the dismissal of the Proskomedia, the Priest censes as the Deacon: from the solea - the iconostasis, both choirs and the people (see nos. 33 and 75). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 173. The prayers of the Great and Small Litanies the Priest recites before the exclamation, and not after it. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 174. For the Little Entrance, the Priest takes the Holy Gospel, goes around the throne from the right side and goes out through the northern doors. Bowing his head before the holy doors, he recites the Prayer of the Entrance "O Master, Lord our God," blesses the entrance with His right hand, kisses the Gospel and, showing it, exclaims: "Wisdom, arise." After this He enters the sanctuary through the holy doors (see no. 122, note). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 175. When the Choir finishes singing the Thrice-Holy Hymn, the Priest exclaims: "Let us be attentive" and blesses: "Peace be to all. Wisdom, let us be attentive." The Choir sings the Prokimenon. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 176. Having recited the Prayer of the Cherubic Hymn, the Priest blesses the incense, after which he then censes around the holy table, the icon behind the altar, the oblation and from the solea - the iconostasis, both choirs and the people. Having returned to the throne and holding the censer on the ring finger of his left hand, he recites, having lifted his hands, three times the Cherubic Hymn. Then he approaches the oblation, with three swings censes the Holy Gifts, saying quietly: "O God, be merciful to me a sinner." Then He takes the large veil and places it on His own left shoulder, saying: "Lift up your hands," takes the holy diskos - with the left, and the chalice - with the right hand and goes out through the northern doors, exclaiming: "May the Lord God remember" (see no. 129). Having entered through the holy doors, He places the Holy Gifts on the throne, as is proper, censes the large veil over the censer, which the server presents, saying: "The noble Joseph." Then, having taken the censer from the hands of the server, he censes the Holy Gifts with three swings, saying: "Do good, O Lord." (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 177. Having given away the censer, he exclaims the litany "Let us complete," "Help us, save us, have mercy." Reciting the Symbol of Faith he lifts the aer and holds it over the Holy Gifts. According to the prescription, exclaiming "Singing the triumphant hymn," he strikes the diskos with the star-cover as the Deacon does, kisses it and lays it aside on the holy table (see no. 133). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 178. The Priest, having gathered all particles that were on the diskos into the chalice, covers it with the purificator or veil, or even both, places the folded star-cover, small veil and aer on the diskos. Having drawn open the curtain of the iconostasis and opened the holy doors, he places the spoon on the chalice or puts it into the chalice and, having made a small bow, takes the holy chalice in his hands; approaches the holy doors and, showing the chalice, exclaims: "With fear." Immediately after this he recites: "I believe, O Lord, and I confess." After the completion of the prayers, He covers the chalice and administers communion with the spoon. Having finished the communion of the faithful, he leaves the spoon in the chalice, covers it with the purificator and veil and, blessing the people, exclaims: "O God, save Thy people." Having returned to the holy table, He places the chalice and censes it with three swings, saying: "Be Thou exalted." Having made a small bow, He takes with the right hand the holy chalice, and with the left - the holy diskos, having said first quietly: "Blessed is our God," and then loudly: "Always, now and ever" and, turning to the people, goes to the table of oblation, on which He places everything. Having made after this a small bow, He returns to the holy table (see no. 142). (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 179. One may also do thus: having communicated, the Priest places the holy diskos on the holy chalice and covers it with the small veil or purificator, or even both. Having made a small bow, he takes the holy chalice and, showing it to the people, exclaims: "With fear." Having returned to the holy table, he takes the chalice and diskos between the index and middle fingers, takes the spoon with the right hand and begins to communicate the faithful. After the completion of communion, he leaves the spoon in the chalice, returns to the holy table and places the diskos and chalice on it, covers it with the small veil. After this he takes the chalice and, turning to the people, blesses them with the chalice, saying: "O God, save Thy people." Having placed the chalice on the holy table, He censes it with three swings, saying: "Be Thou exalted." Then He places the purificator under the small veil, with which the chalice is covered, and places the folded star-cover and the second small veil and aer on the holy diskos, makes a small bow, takes the holy chalice and, having turned to the holy doors, looking at the people, says quietly: "Blessed is our God"; after this exclaims: "Always, now and ever." Then He takes from the throne with His left hand the diskos and goes to the table of oblation, on which He leaves everything, and after a small bow returns to the holy table. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 180. The Priest, exclaiming the thanksgiving litany "Arise! Having partaken," the prayer and the exclamation "For Thou Art our sanctification," folds the antimension [iliton] according to the prescription. After the dismissal He returns to the sanctuary and closes the holy doors. At the end He goes to the table of oblation and consumes there the Holy Gifts with fear and all caution, washes His hands and, having thanked God, departs. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 4. Rubric of the Non-Solemn Divine Liturgy, that is, served ordinarily (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 181. All rites and ceremonies which we perform at the solemn Liturgy must be preserved also in the ordinary Liturgy, whether, as it is said, in the private Liturgy. What, according to the prescription, is to be exclaimed, let it be exclaimed so that it can be heard not only by the Reader or singer, that is, the one who serves, but also by those present. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 182. The Reader, if possible, should wear a sticharion. His usual place is outside the sanctuary. He stands directly on the right or on the left side of the iconostasis. If it is necessary to enter the sanctuary, for example, to give the Priest the censer or warm water, he may enter only through the side doors. The Priest closes and opens the holy doors, and draws the curtain shut and open himself. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 183. The use of the censer in Liturgies which we celebrate without any solemnity is not absolutely prescribed, but it will be very praiseworthy to use it. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 184. If there is a lack of time for reciting all the preparatory prayers before the iconostasis, let the Priest never omit the prayer "O Lord, stretch forth Thy hand." Having entered the sanctuary, let Him read the psalm "I will enter into Thy house" and, having kissed the holy table, the Gospel and, if there exists a custom, - the altar cross, puts on the sacred vestments. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 185. The Proskomedia should be celebrated not in the sacristy, but on the side altar [table of oblation] which is located near the holy table. The custom of using five prosphora is not necessary, on the contrary, it is permitted to use only one prosphora at several liturgies, even if the sign of the Lamb on the prescribed side is not imprinted, however, at every Liturgy the Priest is obliged to cut the bread and take out particles as if the bread were whole, not using dried lambs and not using either the Lamb or particles cut earlier by the one who serves. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 186. The Little Entrance with the Gospel is done according to the prescriptions, going around the holy table and going out of the sanctuary through the northern doors. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 187. After the Prayer of the Trisagion, the Priest may omit the blessing of the High Throne. Turned to the west, he listens to the reading of the Apostle, having lowered the phelonion and hands under it. He may read the Gospel also without an analogion, when the server, if there exists a custom, supports the book with his head before the holy doors. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 188. The Great Entrance is done according to the prescriptions, going out through the northern doors. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 189. The Priest does not fan over the Holy Gifts, unless there arises a danger that something might fall into the chalice. He does not pour warm water, if he cannot do this conveniently. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 190. He recites the Prayer behind the Ambo before the holy doors, and when he blesses and makes the dismissal, he stands in the very holy doors. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 191. After the dismissal he does not recite any other prayer, but immediately closes the holy doors. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 5. With Concelebrants (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 192. Actual concelebrants are priests who, with the principal celebrant, exclaim the words of consecration, and non-actual, or ordinary assistants - those who do not exclaim the words of consecration. Nothing prevents, if at a solemn concelebration, however not at an ordinary Liturgy, assisting priests join the actual concelebrants. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 193. Actual concelebrants, whether at a solemn Liturgy or at a private Liturgy, celebrate for the first accepted intention. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 194. Priests who actually concelebrate are obliged to recite all the priestly prayers as well as their endings, that is the exclamations, up to the very Prayer of Thanksgiving "We thank Thee" after communion inclusive. And the litanies each either exclaims Himself, according to the prescribed order, or only listens, recites the prayers when the Deacon or another Priest exclaims the litany. Priests who only assist are not obliged to read the priestly prayers, for the principal celebrant is to recite them, but only to say the exclamations in turn. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
  H5: ##### 195. The rites and blessings are performed only by the principal celebrant himself. Bows are made by all together, they lift their hands where it is prescribed and can be done conveniently. (under ### 6.1.1 V. Rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom & St. Basil)
